Implement the following plan:

# Add `--manifest-path` flag with `--mp` alias

## Context

tspec currently requires `cd`ing into a project to operate on it. Adding
`--manifest-path` (with `visible_alias = "mp"`) lets users run
`tspec build --mp /path/to/project` from anywhere, matching cargo's own
`--manifest-path` convention but with a shorter alias.

## Approach: thread `project_root` through internal APIs

The core insight: `project_root` is already computed in `main.rs` and passed
to every command's `execute()`. But two helpers re-derive it from cwd:

- `resolve_package_arg()` calls `find_project_root()` internally
- `current_package_name()` uses `std::env::current_dir()`
- `WorkspaceInfo::discover()` runs `cargo metadata` from cwd

**Fix:** refactor these three to accept `project_root` as a parameter, then
wire `--manifest-path` into `main.rs` to override `find_project_root()`.

## Steps (recommend multi-step: dev1 = refactor, dev2 = add flag)

### Step 1 (dev1): Refactor internals to accept `project_root`

No behavior change — pure refactor.

**`src/cmd/mod.rs`:**
- `resolve_package_arg(pkg, project_root)` — drop internal `find_project_root()` call
- `current_package_name(project_root)` — use project_root instead of cwd;
  if cwd is outside project_root, return None (all-packages mode)

**`src/workspace.rs`:**
- `WorkspaceInfo::discover(project_root)` — pass
  `manifest_path(project_root.join("Cargo.toml"))` to `MetadataCommand`

**Callers (add `project_root` arg):**
- `src/cmd/build.rs` — `resolve_package_arg(pkg, project_root)`, `current_package_name(project_root)`, `WorkspaceInfo::discover(project_root)`
- `src/cmd/run.rs` — same pattern
- `src/cmd/test.rs` — same pattern
- `src/cmd/compare.rs` — same pattern
- `src/cmd/clean.rs` — `resolve_package_arg(pkg, project_root)`
- `src/cmd/clippy.rs` — `resolve_package_arg(pkg, project_root)`
- `src/cmd/fmt.rs` — `resolve_package_arg(pkg, project_root)`
- `src/ts_cmd/show.rs` — `WorkspaceInfo::discover(project_root)`
- `src/ts_cmd/list.rs` — `WorkspaceInfo::discover(project_root)`
- `src/ts_cmd/hash.rs` — `WorkspaceInfo::discover(project_root)`

### Step 2 (dev2): Add `--manifest-path` / `--mp` to CLI

**`src/cli.rs`:**
```rust
/// Path to Cargo.toml or directory containing one
#[arg(long = "manifest-path", visible_alias = "mp", global = true, value_name = "PATH")]
pub manifest_path: Option<PathBuf>,
```

**`src/find_paths.rs`:**
Add `resolve_manifest_path(path: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf>`:
- If path points to a file (Cargo.toml), use its parent
- If path is a directory, use it directly
- Verify Cargo.toml exists
- Then walk up to find workspace root (reuse `find_project_root` logic
  but starting from the given dir instead of cwd)

**`src/main.rs`:**
```rust
let project_root = match cli.manifest_path {
    Some(ref path) => resolve_manifest_path(path)?,
    None => find_project_root()?,
};
```

## Verification

```bash
tspec test -p tspec
tspec test -p tspec-build
tspec install --path .
tspec test -p tspec            # with new binary
tspec clippy
tspec fmt --check
```

Manual smoke tests:
```bash
cd /tmp && tspec build --manifest-path /path/to/tspec
cd /tmp && tspec build --mp /path/to/tspec
cd /tmp && tspec build --mp /path/to/tspec/Cargo.toml
cd /tmp && tspec test --mp /path/to/tspec -p tspec
```

```
    15→pub use compare::CompareCmd;
    16→pub use fmt::FmtCmd;
    17→pub use install::InstallCmd;

    19→pub use test::{TestCmd, TestResult, parse_test_results};
    20→pub use ts::TsCmd;
    21→pub use version::VersionCmd;

    23→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
    24→use std::ffi::OsString;
    25→use std::path::Path;
    26→use std::process::ExitCode;
    27→
    28→use crate::find_paths::{find_package_dir, find_project_root, get_package_name, is_pop};
    29→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    30→
    31→/// Trait for command execution.
    32→pub trait Execute {
    33→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    34→}
    35→
    36→/// Resolve a `-p` argument (path or name) to the actual cargo package name.
    37→/// Returns Some(name) for a package, None if it resolves to a workspace root
    38→/// with no `[package]` section (meaning "operate on all packages").
    39→pub(crate) fn resolve_package_arg(pkg: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    40→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    41→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg)?;
    42→    match get_package_name(&pkg_dir) {
    43→        Ok(name) => Ok(Some(name)),
    44→        Err(_) => Ok(None),
    45→    }
    46→}
    47→
    48→/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.
    49→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(
    50→    subcommand: &str,
    51→    args: &[OsString],
    52→    project_root: &Path,
    53→    flags: &CargoFlags,
    54→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    55→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    56→    cmd.arg(subcommand);
    57→    cmd.args(args);
    58→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
    59→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
    60→    let status = cmd
    61→        .status()
    62→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", subcommand))?;
    63→    if status.success() {
    64→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    65→    } else {
    66→        bail!("cargo {} failed", subcommand);
    67→    }
    68→}
    69→
    70→/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package]).
    71→/// Returns None at a workspace root so that commands default to all-packages mode.
    72→pub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {
    73→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
    74→    // At a workspace root, don't treat it as a single package
    75→    if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() && !is_pop(&cwd) {
    76→        return None;
    77→    }
    78→    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()
    79→}
    80→
```

```
     1→//! Workspace discovery
     2→//!
     3→//! Uses `cargo metadata` to discover workspace members.
     4→
     5→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     6→use cargo_metadata::MetadataCommand;
     7→use std::path::PathBuf;
     8→
     9→/// Information about a workspace package
    10→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    11→pub struct PackageMember {
    12→    pub name: String,
    13→    pub path: PathBuf,
    14→    pub has_binary: bool,
    15→}
    16→
    17→/// Workspace information from cargo metadata
    18→pub struct WorkspaceInfo {
    19→    pub root: PathBuf,
    20→    pub members: Vec<PackageMember>,
    21→}
    22→
    23→impl WorkspaceInfo {
    24→    /// Workspace name derived from the root directory basename
    25→    pub fn name(&self) -> &str {
    26→        self.root
    27→            .file_name()
    28→            .and_then(|n| n.to_str())
    29→            .unwrap_or("workspace")
    30→    }
    31→
    32→    /// Discover workspace using cargo metadata
    33→    pub fn discover() -> Result<Self> {
    34→        let metadata = MetadataCommand::new()
    35→            .no_deps()
    36→            .exec()
    37→            .context("failed to run cargo metadata")?;
    38→
    39→        let root = metadata.workspace_root.as_std_path().to_path_buf();
    40→
    41→        let members: Vec<PackageMember> = metadata
    42→            .workspace_packages()
    43→            .iter()
    44→            .map(|pkg| {
    45→                let path = pkg
    46→                    .manifest_path
    47→                    .parent()
    48→                    .unwrap()
    49→                    .as_std_path()
    50→                    .to_path_buf();
    51→                let has_binary = pkg.targets.iter().any(|t| t.is_bin());
    52→
    53→                PackageMember {
    54→                    name: pkg.name.clone(),
    55→                    path,
    56→                    has_binary,
    57→                }
    58→            })
    59→            .collect();
    60→
    61→        Ok(WorkspaceInfo { root, members })
    62→    }
    63→
    64→    /// Get all workspace members
    65→    pub fn buildable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    66→        self.members.iter().collect()
    67→    }
    68→
    69→    /// Get members that have binaries
    70→    pub fn runnable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    71→        self.members.iter().filter(|m| m.has_binary).collect()
    72→    }
    73→}
    74→
    75→#[cfg(test)]
    76→mod tests {
    77→    use super::*;
    78→
    79→    #[test]
    80→    fn discover_works() {
    81→        // This test works for both workspaces and POPs
    82→        let info = WorkspaceInfo::discover();
    83→        if let Ok(info) = info {
    84→            assert!(!info.members.is_empty());
    85→
    86→            // All members should be buildable
    87→            assert_eq!(info.buildable_members().len(), info.members.len());
    88→
    89→            let _runnable = info.runnable_members();
    90→            // Just verify it doesn't panic - count depends on project structure
    91→        }
    92→        // If cargo metadata fails (e.g., not in a Rust project), that's OK for CI
    93→    }
    94→}
    95→
```

```
    10→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    11→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    12→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    13→
    14→/// Build package(s) with a translation spec
    15→#[derive(Args)]
    16→pub struct BuildCmd {
    17→    /// Package to build (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    18→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    19→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    20→    /// Package to build (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    21→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    22→    pub package: Option<String>,
    23→    /// Build all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    24→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    25→    pub workspace: bool,
    26→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to build with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    27→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    28→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    29→    /// Release build
    30→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    31→    pub release: bool,
    32→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    33→    #[arg(long)]
    34→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    35→    /// Strip symbols from binary after build
    36→    #[arg(short, long)]
    37→    pub strip: bool,
    38→    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    39→    #[arg(short, long)]
    40→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    41→}
    42→
    43→impl BuildCmd {
    44→    /// Resolve the effective profile from --release / --profile flags.
    45→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    46→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    47→            Some(p)
    48→        } else if self.release {
    49→            Some("release")
    50→        } else {
    51→            None
    52→        }
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→impl Execute for BuildCmd {
    57→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    58→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    59→
    60→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    61→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    62→            None
    63→        } else {
    64→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    65→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    66→                None => current_package_name(),
    67→            }
    68→        };
    69→
    70→        match resolved {
    71→            None => {
    72→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    73→                let results = build_all(
    74→                    &workspace,
    75→                    &self.tspec,
    76→                    cli_profile,
    77→                    self.strip,
    78→                    self.fail_fast,
    79→                    flags,
    80→                );
    81→                Ok(print_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    82→            }
    83→            Some(name) => {
    84→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    85→                    let result = build_package(&name, None, cli_profile, flags)?;
    86→                    if self.strip {
    87→                        strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    88→                    }
    89→                } else {
    90→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    91→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    92→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    93→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    94→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    95→                        let result = build_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, flags)?;
    96→                        if self.strip {
    97→                            strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    98→                        }
    99→                    }
   100→                }
   101→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   102→            }
   103→        }
   104→    }
   105→}
```

```
    10→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};

    14→
    15→/// Build and run package(s) with a translation spec
    16→#[derive(Args)]
    17→pub struct RunCmd {
    18→    /// Package to run (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    19→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    20→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    21→    /// Package to run (defaults to current directory or all apps)
    22→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    23→    pub package: Option<String>,
    24→    /// Run all workspace apps (even when in a package directory)
    25→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    26→    pub workspace: bool,
    27→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to run with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    28→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    29→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    30→    /// Release build
    31→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    32→    pub release: bool,
    33→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    34→    #[arg(long)]
    35→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    36→    /// Strip symbols from binary before running
    37→    #[arg(short, long)]
    38→    pub strip: bool,
    39→    /// Arguments to pass to the binary (after --)
    40→    #[arg(last = true)]
    41→    pub args: Vec<String>,
    42→}
    43→
    44→impl RunCmd {
    45→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    46→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    47→            Some(p)
    48→        } else if self.release {
    49→            Some("release")
    50→        } else {
    51→            None
    52→        }
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→impl Execute for RunCmd {
    57→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    58→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    59→
    60→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    61→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    62→            None
    63→        } else {
    64→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    65→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    66→                None => current_package_name(),
    67→            }
    68→        };
    69→
    70→        match resolved {
    71→            None => {
    72→                // Run all apps (args not supported for --workspace)
    73→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    74→                let results = run_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.strip, flags);
    75→                Ok(print_run_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    76→            }
    77→            Some(name) => {
    78→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    79→                    // Build, optionally strip, then run
    80→                    let result = build_package(&name, None, cli_profile, flags)?;
    81→                    if self.strip {
    82→                        strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    83→                    }
    84→                    let exit_code = run_binary(&result.binary_path, &self.args)?;
    85→                    std::process::exit(exit_code);
    86→                } else {
    87→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    88→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    89→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    90→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    91→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    92→                        let result = build_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, flags)?;
    93→                        if self.strip {
    94→                            strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    95→                        }
    96→                        let exit_code = run_binary(&result.binary_path, &self.args)?;
    97→                        if exit_code != 0 {
    98→                            std::process::exit(exit_code);
    99→                        }
   100→                    }
   101→                    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   102→                }
   103→            }
   104→        }
   105→    }
   106→}
```

```
     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};
     7→use crate::all::{print_test_summary, test_all};
     8→use crate::cargo_build::test_package;
     9→use crate::find_paths::{find_project_root, find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    10→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    11→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    12→
    13→/// Parsed test statistics from cargo test output.
    14→#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone)]
    15→pub struct TestResult {
    16→    pub passed: u32,
    17→    pub failed: u32,
    18→    pub ignored: u32,
    19→    pub filtered: u32,
    20→}
    21→
    22→impl TestResult {
    23→    /// Aggregate another TestResult into this one.
    24→    pub fn merge(&mut self, other: &TestResult) {
    25→        self.passed += other.passed;
    26→        self.failed += other.failed;
    27→        self.ignored += other.ignored;
    28→        self.filtered += other.filtered;
    29→    }
    30→
    31→    /// Total tests that actually ran (passed + failed).
    32→    pub fn total_ran(&self) -> u32 {
    33→        self.passed + self.failed
    34→    }
    35→}
    36→
    37→/// Parse a `test result:` line from cargo test output.
    38→///
    39→/// Example: `test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.42s`
    40→pub fn parse_test_result_line(line: &str) -> Option<TestResult> {
    41→    let rest = line.strip_prefix("test result:")?;
    42→    let rest = rest.trim();
    43→    // Skip the "ok." or "FAILED." prefix
    44→    let rest = rest
    45→        .strip_prefix("ok.")
    46→        .or_else(|| rest.strip_prefix("FAILED."))?;
    47→    let rest = rest.trim();
    48→
    49→    let mut result = TestResult::default();
    50→    for part in rest.split(';') {
    51→        let part = part.trim();
    52→        if let Some((num, label)) = part.split_once(' ') {
    53→            let Ok(n) = num.parse::<u32>() else {
    54→                continue;
    55→            };
    56→            match label.trim() {
    57→                "passed" => result.passed = n,
    58→                "failed" => result.failed = n,
    59→                "ignored" => result.ignored = n,
    60→                "filtered" | "filtered out" => result.filtered = n,
    61→                _ => {} // "measured"
    62→            }
    63→        }
    64→    }
    65→    Some(result)
    66→}
    67→
    68→/// Parse and aggregate raw `test result:` lines into a single TestResult.
    69→pub fn parse_test_results(lines: &[String]) -> TestResult {
    70→    let mut aggregated = TestResult::default();
    71→    for line in lines {
    72→        if let Some(parsed) = parse_test_result_line(line) {
    73→            aggregated.merge(&parsed);
    74→        }
    75→    }
    76→    aggregated
    77→}
    78→
    79→/// Test package(s) with a translation spec
    80→#[derive(Args)]
    81→pub struct TestCmd {
    82→    /// Package to test (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    83→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    84→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    85→    /// Package to test (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    86→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    87→    pub package: Option<String>,
    88→    /// Test all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    89→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    90→    pub workspace: bool,
    91→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to test with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    92→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    93→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    94→    /// Release build
    95→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    96→    pub release: bool,
    97→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    98→    #[arg(long)]
    99→    pub profile: Option<String>,
   100→    /// Stop on first failure
   101→    #[arg(short, long)]
   102→    pub fail_fast: bool,
   103→    /// List test targets and test functions
   104→    #[arg(short, long)]
   105→    pub list: bool,
   106→    /// List available target names for use with --test
   107→    #[arg(long = "target-names")]
   108→    pub target_names: bool,
   109→    /// Filter by qualified test name, e.g. '-n _by_' matches 'ts_cmd::remove::tests::remove_by_index'
   110→    #[arg(short = 'n', long = "names", num_args = 1..)]
   111→    pub name_filter: Vec<String>,
   112→    /// Run only the named test target (use --target-names to see available names; repeatable)
   113→    #[arg(long = "test", value_name = "TARGET-NAME")]
   114→    pub test_target: Vec<String>,
   115→    /// Include #[ignore]'d tests (equivalent to -- --include-ignored)
   116→    #[arg(long = "all-tests")]
   117→    pub all_tests: bool,
   118→    /// Extra arguments passed after -- to the test binary (e.g., --ignored)
   119→    #[arg(last = true)]
   120→    pub test_args: Vec<String>,
   121→}
   122→
   123→impl TestCmd {
   124→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
   125→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
   126→            Some(p)
   127→        } else if self.release {
   128→            Some("release")
   129→        } else {
   130→            None
   131→        }
   132→    }
   133→}
   134→
   135→impl Execute for TestCmd {
   136→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   137→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
   138→
   139→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
   140→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
   141→            None
   142→        } else {
   143→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
   144→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
   145→                None => current_package_name(),
   146→            }
   147→        };
   148→
   149→        if self.target_names {
   150→            return list_target_names(resolved.as_deref(), flags);
   151→        }
   152→
   153→        if self.list {
   154→            return list_tests(resolved.as_deref(), &self.name_filter, flags);
   155→        }
   156→
   157→        // Build extra_args from --names, --test, --all-tests, and trailing args
   158→        let has_extra = !self.name_filter.is_empty()
   159→            || !self.test_target.is_empty()
   160→            || !self.test_args.is_empty()
   161→            || self.all_tests;
   162→        let flags = if has_extra {
   163→            let mut f = flags.clone();
   164→            for target in &self.test_target {
   165→                f.extra_args.push("--test".to_string());
   166→                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());
   167→            }
   168→            // Name filters, --all-tests, and test_args all go after --
   169→            // The test harness accepts [FILTERS...] as OR-matched substrings
   170→            if !self.name_filter.is_empty() || !self.test_args.is_empty() || self.all_tests {
   171→                f.extra_args.push("--".to_string());
   172→                f.extra_args.extend(self.name_filter.clone());
   173→                if self.all_tests {
   174→                    f.extra_args.push("--include-ignored".to_string());
   175→                }
   176→                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());
   177→            }
   178→            f
   179→        } else {
   180→            flags.clone()
   181→        };
   182→
   183→        match resolved {
   184→            None => {
   185→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
   186→
   187→                // When --test targets are specified in workspace mode, only test
   188→                // packages that actually have matching files in tests/
   189→                if !self.test_target.is_empty() {
   190→                    let members = workspace.buildable_members();
   191→                    let matching: Vec<_> = members
   192→                        .iter()
   193→                        .filter(|m| {
   194→                            self.test_target
   195→                                .iter()
   196→                                .any(|t| m.path.join("tests").join(format!("{t}.rs")).exists())
   197→                        })
   198→                        .collect();
   199→
   200→                    if matching.is_empty() {
   201→                        eprintln!(
   202→                            "No packages contain test targets: {}",
   203→                            self.test_target.join(", ")
   204→                        );
   205→                        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   206→                    }
   207→
   208→                    for member in &matching {
   209→                        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   210→                        test_package(&member.name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
   211→                    }
   212→                    return Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS);
   213→                }
   214→
   215→                let results =
   216→                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);
   217→                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
   218→            }
   219→            Some(name) => {
   220→                let mut all_lines = Vec::new();
   221→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
   222→                    all_lines = test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
   223→                } else {
   224→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
   225→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
   226→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
   227→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
   228→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
   229→                        let lines = test_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, &flags)?;
   230→                        all_lines.extend(lines);
   231→                    }
   232→                }
   233→                let counts = parse_test_results(&all_lines);
   234→                if counts.total_ran() == 0 {
   235→                    eprintln!("Error: 0 tests ran (filter may not match anything)");
   236→                    return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   237→                }
   238→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   239→            }
   240→        }
   241→    }
   242→}
   243→
   244→/// Format a cargo test target header into a human-friendly label.
   245→///
   246→/// Cargo emits headers like:
   247→/// - `unittests src/lib.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec-abc123)` → unit tests (no --test name)
   248→/// - `tests/tspec_test.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec_test-abc123)` → integration test
   249→///
   250→/// For integration tests, we show the `--test` usable name prominently.
   251→fn format_target_header(raw: &str) -> String {
   252→    // Strip the "(target/...)" suffix for cleaner output
   253→    let label = if let Some(idx) = raw.find(" (") {
   254→        &raw[..idx]
   255→    } else {
   256→        raw
   257→    };
   258→
   259→    if label.starts_with("unittests ") {
   260→        // "unittests src/lib.rs" → unit tests, not selectable with --test
   261→        format!("unit tests ({})", label.strip_prefix("unittests ").unwrap())
   262→    } else if label.starts_with("tests/") {
   263→        // "tests/tspec_test.rs" → extract basename for --test
   264→        let basename = label
   265→            .strip_prefix("tests/")
   266→            .and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(".rs"))
   267→            .unwrap_or(label);
   268→        format!("{} (--test {})", label, basename)
   269→    } else {
   270→        label.to_string()
   271→    }
   272→}
   273→
   274→/// List available target names for `--test`.
   275→///
   276→/// Runs `cargo test --no-run` and parses stderr for "Running tests/..." lines,
   277→/// extracting the basename (without .rs) as the target name.
   278→fn list_target_names(package: Option<&str>, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   279→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   280→
   281→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
   282→    cmd.arg("test").arg("--no-run");
   283→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   284→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   285→    }
   286→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   287→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   288→
   289→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test --no-run")?;
   290→
   291→    if !output.status.success() {
   292→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   293→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   294→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   295→    }
   296→
   297→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   298→    let mut found = false;
   299→
   300→    println!("Available target names for --test:");
   301→    for line in stderr.lines() {
   302→        let trimmed = line.trim();
   303→        // --no-run emits "Executable tests/foo.rs (target/...)" for integration tests
   304→        if let Some(rest) = trimmed
   305→            .strip_prefix("Executable tests/")
   306→            .or_else(|| trimmed.strip_prefix("Running tests/"))
   307→        {
   308→            let file = if let Some(idx) = rest.find(" (") {
   309→                &rest[..idx]
   310→            } else {
   311→                rest
   312→            };
   313→            if let Some(name) = file.strip_suffix(".rs") {
   314→                println!("  {}    (tests/{})", name, file);
   315→                found = true;
   316→            }
   317→        }
   318→    }
   319→
   320→    if !found {
   321→        println!("  (none — no integration test files in tests/)");
   322→    }
   323→
   324→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   325→}
   326→
   327→/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output.
   328→///
   329→/// Groups test functions under their target headers, showing counts per target
   330→/// and a total. Skips targets with zero tests.
   331→fn list_tests(
   332→    package: Option<&str>,
   333→    name_filter: &[String],
   334→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   335→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   336→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   337→
   338→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
   339→    cmd.arg("test");
   340→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   341→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   342→    }
   343→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   344→    cmd.arg("--");
   345→    cmd.args(name_filter);
   346→    cmd.arg("--list");
   347→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   348→
   349→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test -- --list")?;
   350→
   351→    if !output.status.success() {
   352→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   353→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   354→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   355→    }
   356→
   357→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   358→    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
   359→
   360→    // Parse target headers from stderr ("Running ..." and "Doc-tests ..." lines)
   361→    // Produce human-friendly labels that show the --test name when applicable.
   362→    let targets: Vec<String> = stderr
   363→        .lines()
   364→        .filter_map(|line| {
   365→            let trimmed = line.trim();
   366→            if trimmed.starts_with("Running ") {
   367→                let rest = trimmed.strip_prefix("Running ").unwrap();
   368→                Some(format_target_header(rest))
   369→            } else if trimmed.starts_with("Doc-tests ") {
   370→                Some(trimmed.to_string())
   371→            } else {
   372→                None
   373→            }
   374→        })
   375→        .collect();
   376→
   377→    // Parse stdout into groups split by "N tests, M benchmarks" summary lines
   378→    let mut groups: Vec<Vec<&str>> = Vec::new();
   379→    let mut current: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
   380→    for line in stdout.lines() {
   381→        if line.contains("tests,") && line.contains("benchmarks") {
   382→            groups.push(std::mem::take(&mut current));
   383→        } else if line.ends_with(": test") || line.ends_with(": bench") {
   384→            current.push(line);
   385→        }
   386→    }
   387→
   388→    // Print grouped output
   389→    let mut total = 0;
   390→    for (i, tests) in groups.iter().enumerate() {
   391→        if tests.is_empty() {
   392→            continue;
   393→        }
   394→        let unknown = "unknown target".to_string();
   395→        let header = targets.get(i).unwrap_or(&unknown);
   396→        println!("{} ({} tests)", header, tests.len());
   397→        for test in tests {
   398→            // Strip ": test" / ": bench" suffix for cleaner output
   399→            let name = test
   400→                .strip_suffix(": test")
   401→                .or_else(|| test.strip_suffix(": bench"))
   402→                .unwrap_or(test);
   403→            println!("  {}", name);
   404→        }
   405→        total += tests.len();
   406→        println!();
   407→    }
   408→
   409→    println!("{} tests total", total);
   410→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   411→}
```

```
     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};
     7→use crate::all::{compare_all, print_compare_summary};
     8→use crate::compare::{compare_specs, print_comparison};
     9→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    10→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    11→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    12→
    13→/// Compare specs for a package (size only)
    14→#[derive(Args)]
    15→pub struct CompareCmd {
    16→    /// Package to compare (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    17→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    18→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    19→    /// Package to compare (defaults to current directory)
    20→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    21→    pub package: Option<String>,
    22→    /// Compare all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    24→    pub workspace: bool,
    25→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) (defaults to tspec* pattern)
    26→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    27→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    28→    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    29→    #[arg(short, long)]
    30→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    31→}
    32→
    33→impl Execute for CompareCmd {
    34→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    35→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    36→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    37→            None
    38→        } else {
    39→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    40→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    41→                None => current_package_name(),
    42→            }
    43→        };
    44→
    45→        match resolved {
    46→            None => {
    47→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    48→                let results = compare_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, self.fail_fast, flags);
    49→                Ok(print_compare_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    50→            }
    51→            Some(pkg_name) => {
    52→                let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&pkg_name))?;
    53→                let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    54→                let spec_paths = if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    55→                    find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec).unwrap_or_default()
    56→                } else {
    57→                    find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?
    58→                };
    59→                let results = compare_specs(&pkg_name, &spec_paths, flags)?;
    60→                print_comparison(&pkg_name, &results);
    61→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    62→            }
    63→        }
    64→    }
    65→}

   154→        let cmd = parse(&["--workspace", "--fail-fast"]);
   155→        assert!(cmd.workspace);
   156→        assert!(cmd.fail_fast);
```

```
    10→/// Clean build artifacts
    11→#[derive(Args)]
    12→pub struct CleanCmd {
    13→    /// Package to clean (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    14→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    15→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    16→    /// Package to clean (defaults to entire workspace/project)
    17→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    18→    pub package: Option<String>,
    19→    /// Only clean release artifacts
    20→    #[arg(short, long)]
    21→    pub release: bool,
    22→}
    23→

    25→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    26→        let mut args: Vec<OsString> = Vec::new();
    27→        let pkg_arg = self.positional.as_ref().or(self.package.as_ref());
    28→        if let Some(pkg) = pkg_arg

    30→        {

    32→            args.push(name.into());

    35→            args.push("--release".into());
    36→        }
    37→        execute_cargo_subcommand("clean", &args, project_root, flags)
    38→    }
    39→}
```

```
     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use glob::Pattern;
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::{Spec, profile_dir_name};
     7→
     8→/// Check if TOML content has an exact section header like `[workspace]` or `[package]`
     9→/// Only matches lines where the trimmed content equals `[section]` exactly
    10→fn has_toml_section_exact(content: &str, section: &str) -> bool {
    11→    let header = format!("[{}]", section);
    12→    content.lines().any(|line| line.trim() == header)
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Extract package name from Cargo.toml
    16→pub fn get_package_name(crate_dir: &Path) -> Result<String> {
    17→    let cargo_toml = crate_dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    18→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)
    19→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml.display()))?;
    20→
    21→    // Simple parsing - look for name = "..." in [package] section
    22→    let mut in_package = false;
    23→    for line in content.lines() {
    24→        let line = line.trim();
    25→        if line == "[package]" {
    26→            in_package = true;
    27→            continue;
    28→        }
    29→        if line.starts_with('[') {
    30→            in_package = false;
    31→            continue;
    32→        }
    33→        if in_package
    34→            && line.starts_with("name")
    35→            && let Some(eq_pos) = line.find('=')
    36→        {
    37→            let value = line[eq_pos + 1..].trim();
    38→            let value = value.trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'');
    39→            return Ok(value.to_string());
    40→        }
    41→    }
    42→    bail!("could not find package name in {}", cargo_toml.display());
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]
    46→/// For workspaces, returns the directory containing the workspace Cargo.toml
    47→/// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml
    48→/// Respects workspace `exclude` — a package inside an excluded path is treated as a POP.
    49→pub fn find_project_root() -> Result<PathBuf> {
    50→    let mut dir = std::env::current_dir()?;
    51→    let mut package_root: Option<PathBuf> = None;
    52→
    53→    loop {
    54→        let cargo_toml = dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    55→        if cargo_toml.exists() {
    56→            let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)?;
    57→            // Workspace takes precedence — unless the package we found is excluded
    58→            if has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace") {
    59→                if let Some(ref pkg_root) = package_root
    60→                    && is_excluded_from_workspace(&content, &dir, pkg_root)
    61→                {
    62→                    return Ok(pkg_root.clone());
    63→                }
    64→                return Ok(dir);
    65→            }
    66→            // Remember the first (deepest) package we find as potential POP root
    67→            if package_root.is_none() && has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package") {
    68→                package_root = Some(dir.clone());
    69→            }
    70→        }
    71→        if !dir.pop() {
    72→            // No workspace found, use the POP root if we found one
    73→            if let Some(root) = package_root {
    74→                return Ok(root);
    75→            }
    76→            bail!("could not find project root (no Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package])");
    77→        }
    78→    }
    79→}
    80→
    81→/// Check if a package directory is excluded from a workspace.
    82→/// `ws_content` is the workspace Cargo.toml content, `ws_dir` is the workspace root,
    83→/// and `pkg_dir` is the package directory to check.
    84→fn is_excluded_from_workspace(ws_content: &str, ws_dir: &Path, pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {
    85→    let Ok(rel) = pkg_dir.strip_prefix(ws_dir) else {
    86→        return false;
    87→    };
    88→    let rel_str = rel.to_string_lossy();
    89→
    90→    // Parse workspace.exclude from the TOML
    91→    let Ok(doc) = ws_content.parse::<toml::Value>() else {
    92→        return false;
    93→    };
    94→    let Some(excludes) = doc
    95→        .get("workspace")
    96→        .and_then(|w| w.get("exclude"))
    97→        .and_then(|e| e.as_array())
    98→    else {
    99→        return false;
   100→    };
   101→
   102→    for exclude in excludes {
   103→        let Some(pattern) = exclude.as_str() else {
   104→            continue;
   105→        };
   106→        // Check if the package path starts with the exclude pattern
   107→        // e.g., exclude = ["tests/fixtures"] should match "tests/fixtures/pop"
   108→        if rel_str == pattern || rel_str.starts_with(&format!("{}/", pattern)) {
   109→            return true;
   110→        }
   111→        // Also try glob matching
   112→        if let Ok(glob) = Pattern::new(pattern)
   113→            && glob.matches(&rel_str)
   114→        {
   115→            return true;
   116→        }
   117→    }
   118→
   119→    false
   120→}
   121→
   122→/// Check if a project root is a POP (Plain Old Package) vs a workspace
   123→pub fn is_pop(project_root: &Path) -> bool {
   124→    let cargo_toml = project_root.join("Cargo.toml");
   125→    if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml) {
   126→        // POP has [package] but no [workspace]
   127→        has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package")
   128→            && !has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace")
   129→    } else {
   130→        false
   131→    }
   132→}
   133→
   134→/// Resolve package directory from optional name, defaulting to current directory
   135→/// If package is None, uses current directory (must contain Cargo.toml)
   136→/// If package is Some, looks up the package by name
   137→pub fn resolve_package_dir(workspace: &Path, package: Option<&str>) -> Result<PathBuf> {
   138→    match package {
   139→        Some(name) => find_package_dir(workspace, name),
   140→        None => {
   141→            let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
   142→            if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   143→                Ok(cwd)
   144→            } else {
   145→                bail!(
   146→                    "not in a package directory (no Cargo.toml found). Use -p to specify a package."
   147→                )
   148→            }
   149→        }
   150→    }
   151→}
   152→
   153→/// Find a package's directory - tries as path first, then searches standard locations
   154→/// For POPs, checks if name matches the root package
   155→pub fn find_package_dir(project_root: &Path, name: &str) -> Result<PathBuf> {
   156→    // Try as path first (relative or absolute)
   157→    let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   158→    if as_path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   159→        return Ok(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   160→    }
   161→
   162→    // Check if name matches the root package
   163→    if let Ok(pkg_name) = get_package_name(project_root)
   164→        && pkg_name == name
   165→    {
   166→        return Ok(project_root.to_path_buf());
   167→    }
   168→
   169→    // For POPs, nothing else to search
   170→    if is_pop(project_root) {
   171→        bail!(
   172→            "package '{}' not found (this is a single-package project with package '{}')",
   173→            name,
   174→            get_package_name(project_root).unwrap_or_else(|_| "unknown".to_string())
   175→        );
   176→    }
   177→
   178→    // Workspace: search root-level members, then libs/, apps/, tools/
   179→    let root_path = project_root.join(name);
   180→    if root_path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   181→        return Ok(root_path);
   182→    }
   183→
   184→    for prefix in ["libs", "apps", "tools"] {
   185→        let path = project_root.join(prefix).join(name);
   186→        if path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   187→            return Ok(path);
   188→        }
   189→    }
   190→
   191→    // Special case: nested test crates like libs/rlibc-x2/tests
   192→    for prefix in ["libs", "apps"] {
   193→        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(project_root.join(prefix))
   194→            .into_iter()
   195→            .flatten()
   196→            .flatten()
   197→        {
   198→            let nested = entry.path().join("tests");
   199→            if nested.join("Cargo.toml").exists()
   200→                && let Ok(pkg_name) = get_package_name(&nested)
   201→                && pkg_name == name
   202→            {
   203→                return Ok(nested);
   204→            }
   205→        }
   206→    }
   207→
   208→    bail!("package '{}' not found", name);
   209→}
   210→
   211→/// Find the tspec for a package - tries as path first, then relative to pkg_dir
   212→/// Returns None if no tspec exists (plain cargo build will be used)
   213→pub fn find_tspec(pkg_dir: &Path, explicit: Option<&str>) -> Result<Option<PathBuf>> {
   214→    match explicit {
   215→        Some(name) => {
   216→            // Try as path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   217→            let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   218→            if as_path.exists() {
   219→                return Ok(Some(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path)));
   220→            }
   221→
   222→            // Fallback: relative to package directory
   223→            let in_pkg = pkg_dir.join(name);
   224→            if in_pkg.exists() {
   225→                return Ok(Some(in_pkg));
   226→            }
   227→
   228→            // Try with TSPEC_SUFFIX if name has no extension
   229→            if !name.contains('.') {
   230→                let with_suffix = pkg_dir.join(format!("{}{}", name, TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   231→                if with_suffix.exists() {
   232→                    return Ok(Some(with_suffix));
   233→                }
   234→            }
   235→
   236→            bail!("tspec not found: {}", name);
   237→        }
   238→        None => {
   239→            let default = pkg_dir.join(format!("tspec{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   240→            if default.exists() {
   241→                Ok(Some(default))
   242→            } else {
   243→                Ok(None) // No tspec = plain cargo build
   244→            }
   245→        }
   246→    }
   247→}
   248→
   249→/// Find multiple tspecs by glob patterns
   250→/// If no patterns given, defaults to "tspec*{TSPEC_SUFFIX}"
   251→/// Returns sorted list of paths, errors if none found
   252→pub fn find_tspecs(pkg_dir: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>> {
   253→    let default_pattern = format!("tspec*{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX);
   254→    let patterns: Vec<&str> = if patterns.is_empty() {
   255→        vec![&default_pattern]
   256→    } else {
   257→        patterns.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
   258→    };
   259→
   260→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   261→
   262→    for pattern in &patterns {
   263→        // Try as literal path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   264→        let as_path = PathBuf::from(pattern);
   265→        if as_path.exists() && as_path.is_file() {
   266→            results.push(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   267→            continue;
   268→        }
   269→
   270→        // Try as literal path relative to pkg_dir
   271→        let in_pkg = pkg_dir.join(pattern);
   272→        if in_pkg.exists() && in_pkg.is_file() {
   273→            results.push(in_pkg);
   274→            continue;
   275→        }
   276→
   277→        // Try as glob pattern in pkg_dir
   278→        let glob_pattern =
   279→            Pattern::new(pattern).with_context(|| format!("invalid glob pattern: {}", pattern))?;
   280→
   281→        let entries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(pkg_dir)
   282→            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read directory: {}", pkg_dir.display()))?
   283→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   284→            .filter(|e| {
   285→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   286→                e.path().is_file() && glob_pattern.matches(&name)
   287→            })
   288→            .map(|e| e.path())
   289→            .collect();
   290→
   291→        results.extend(entries);
   292→    }
   293→
   294→    // Remove duplicates and sort
   295→    results.sort();
   296→    results.dedup();
   297→
   298→    if results.is_empty() {
   299→        let pattern_list = patterns.join(", ");
   300→        bail!(
   301→            "no tspec files found matching '{}' in {}",
   302→            pattern_list,
   303→            pkg_dir.display()
   304→        );
   305→    }
   306→
   307→    Ok(results)
   308→}
   309→
   310→/// Get binary path for a build with a spec.
   311→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   312→pub fn get_binary_path(
   313→    workspace: &Path,
   314→    crate_name: &str,
   315→    spec: &Spec,
   316→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   317→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   318→) -> PathBuf {
   319→    let target = spec.cargo.target_triple.clone().or_else(|| {
   320→        spec.cargo
   321→            .target_json
   322→            .as_ref()
   323→            .and_then(|p| p.file_stem().map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string()))
   324→    });
   325→
   326→    // Determine profile directory: spec profile takes precedence, then CLI profile
   327→    let dir = match spec.cargo.profile.as_deref() {
   328→        Some(p) => profile_dir_name(p),
   329→        None => match cli_profile {
   330→            Some(p) => profile_dir_name(p),
   331→            None => "debug",
   332→        },
   333→    };
   334→
   335→    let base = match expanded_target_dir {
   336→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   337→        None => workspace.join("target"),
   338→    };
   339→
   340→    match target {
   341→        Some(t) => base.join(t).join(dir).join(crate_name),
   342→        None => base.join(dir).join(crate_name),
   343→    }
   344→}
   345→
   346→/// Get binary path for a simple build (no spec).
   347→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   348→pub fn get_binary_path_simple(
   349→    workspace: &Path,
   350→    crate_name: &str,
   351→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   352→) -> PathBuf {
   353→    let dir = match cli_profile {
   354→        Some(p) => profile_dir_name(p),
   355→        None => "debug",
   356→    };
   357→    workspace.join("target").join(dir).join(crate_name)
   358→}

   360→#[cfg(test)]
   361→mod tests {
   362→    use super::*;
   363→    use crate::test_constants::SUFFIX;
   364→    use std::fs;
   365→    use std::path::PathBuf;
   366→    use tempfile::TempDir;
   367→
   368→    // ==================== get_package_name tests ====================
   369→
   370→    #[test]
   371→    fn get_package_name_valid() {
   372→        let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   373→        let cargo_toml = tmp.path().join("Cargo.toml");
   374→        fs::write(
   375→            &cargo_toml,
   376→            r#"[package]
   377→name = "my-test-crate"
   378→version = "0.1.0"
   379→"#,
   380→        )
   381→        .unwrap();
   382→
   383→        let name = get_package_name(tmp.path()).unwrap();
   384→        assert_eq!(name, "my-test-crate");
   385→    }
   386→
   387→    #[test]
   388→    fn get_package_name_with_single_quotes() {
   389→        let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   390→        let cargo_toml = tmp.path().join("Cargo.toml");
   391→        fs::write(
   392→            &cargo_toml,
   393→            "[package]\nname = 'single-quoted'\nversion = '0.1.0'\n",
   394→        )
   395→        .unwrap();
   396→
   397→        let name = get_package_name(tmp.path()).unwrap();
   398→        assert_eq!(name, "single-quoted");
   399→    }
   400→
   401→    #[test]
   402→    fn get_package_name_missing_name() {
   403→        let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   404→        let cargo_toml = tmp.path().join("Cargo.toml");
   405→        fs::write(&cargo_toml, "[package]\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\n").unwrap();
   406→
   407→        let result = get_package_name(tmp.path());
   408→        assert!(result.is_err());
   409→        assert!(
   410→            result
   411→                .unwrap_err()
   412→                .to_string()
   413→                .contains("could not find package name")
   414→        );
   415→    }
   416→
   417→    #[test]

   434→[package]

   619→    #[test]
   620→    fn find_tspecs_default_pattern() {
   621→        let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   622→        let crate_dir = tmp.path().join("crate");
   623→        fs::create_dir(&crate_dir).unwrap();
   624→
   625→        let default_name = format!("tspec{}", SUFFIX);
   626→        let opt_name = format!("tspec-opt{}", SUFFIX);
   627→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&default_name), "# default").unwrap();
   628→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&opt_name), "# opt").unwrap();
   629→        fs::write(crate_dir.join("other.toml"), "# other").unwrap();
   630→
   631→        // Empty patterns = default "tspec*{SUFFIX}"
   632→        let found = find_tspecs(&crate_dir, &[]).unwrap();
   633→        assert_eq!(found.len(), 2);
   634→        assert!(
   635→            found
   636→                .iter()
   637→                .any(|p| p.file_name().unwrap() == default_name.as_str())
   638→        );
   639→        assert!(
   640→            found
   641→                .iter()
   642→                .any(|p| p.file_name().unwrap() == opt_name.as_str())
   643→        );
   644→    }

   679→    fn find_tspecs_sorted_and_deduped() {
   680→        let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   681→        let crate_dir = tmp.path().join("crate");
   682→        fs::create_dir(&crate_dir).unwrap();
   683→
   684→        fs::write(crate_dir.join("z.toml"), "# z").unwrap();
   685→        fs::write(crate_dir.join("a.toml"), "# a").unwrap();
   686→
   687→        // Request same file twice via different patterns
   688→        let found = find_tspecs(&crate_dir, &["*.toml".to_string(), "a.toml".to_string()]).unwrap();
   689→        assert_eq!(found.len(), 2); // Deduped
   690→        assert_eq!(found[0].file_name().unwrap(), "a.toml"); // Sorted
   691→        assert_eq!(found[1].file_name().unwrap(), "z.toml");
   692→    }

   710→    #[test]
   711→    fn find_tspecs_glob_matches_multi_dot_filenames() {
   712→        let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   713→        let crate_dir = tmp.path().join("crate");
   714→        fs::create_dir(&crate_dir).unwrap();
   715→
   716→        let default_name = format!("tspec{}", SUFFIX);
   717→        let musl_name = format!("tspec.musl{}", SUFFIX);
   718→        let other_name = format!("t1{}", SUFFIX);
   719→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&default_name), "# default").unwrap();
   720→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&musl_name), "# musl").unwrap();
   721→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&other_name), "# other").unwrap();
   722→
   723→        // Default pattern should match tspec* but not t1*
   724→        let found = find_tspecs(&crate_dir, &[]).unwrap();
   725→        assert_eq!(found.len(), 2);
   726→        assert!(
   727→            found
   728→                .iter()
   729→                .any(|p| p.file_name().unwrap() == default_name.as_str())
   730→        );
   731→        assert!(
   732→            found
   733→                .iter()
   734→                .any(|p| p.file_name().unwrap() == musl_name.as_str())
   735→        );
   736→
   737→        // Wildcard should match all three
   738→        let found_all = find_tspecs(&crate_dir, &[format!("*{}", SUFFIX)]).unwrap();
   739→        assert_eq!(found_all.len(), 3);
   740→    }
```

```
     1→# Chores 6
     2→
     3→## 20260220 - Parse cargo test output for counts and filtering
     4→
     5→### Problem
     6→
     7→`tspec test` streams cargo output live but has no insight into what
     8→happened. The summary shows only `[PASS]`/`[FAIL]` per package with
     9→no test counts. Worse, 0 tests running reports as success — a filter
    10→typo silently passes. And `--test <target>` emits noisy "running 0
    11→tests" blocks for non-matching targets within a package.
    12→
    13→### Solution
    14→
    15→Tee stdout line-by-line (live output + selective capture), parse
    16→cargo's `test result:` lines for counts, and surface them:
    17→
    18→- Per-package counts in summary: `[PASS]  263 passed`
    19→- Aggregate footer: `Test: 2 packages, 279 passed, 0 failed`
    20→- Fail (exit 1) when 0 tests ran in single-package mode
    21→- Filter "running 0 tests" noise
    22→
    23→Infrastructure in a reusable `tee.rs` module so future commands
    24→(build warnings, benchmarks) can use the same pattern.
    25→
    26→### Plan
    27→
    28→- **dev1:** tee.rs + TestResult + parse_test_result_line + wire into
    29→  run_cargo for test mode. No visible behavior change.
    30→- **dev2:** Summary counts, 0-test failure, noise filtering.
    31→- **dev3:** Fail fixtures + failure-path tests.
    32→
    33→### Decision: fail fixtures for testing failure paths
    34→
    35→Considered external repos vs in-tree fixtures. In-tree is better
    36→for normal development. Fail fixtures (`pop-fail`, `pows-fail`)
    37→live in `tests/fixtures/`, workspace-excluded, with actual failing
    38→tests baked in. Integration tests using them are `#[ignore]`'d —
    39→skipped during normal `tspec test`, run with `-- --ignored`.
    40→
    41→Manual testing: `cd tests/fixtures/pop-fail && tspec test`.
    42→
    43→Also add unit tests for `print_test_summary` with synthetic
    44→OpResult data (pass/fail/mixed with counts, verify exit codes).
    45→
    46→### Result
    47→
    48→- `tee.rs` — reusable tee_stdout(cmd, filter, suppress) utility
    49→- `cargo_build.rs` — run_cargo returns raw matched lines for Test
    50→  mode; noise suppression for "running 0 tests" blocks
    51→- `cmd/test.rs` — TestResult, parse_test_result_line(),
    52→  parse_test_results(); 0-test guard for single-package mode
    53→- `all.rs` — OpResult.test_counts, per-package counts in summary,
    54→  aggregate footer
    55→- 0-test guard only in single-package mode (workspace fixtures
    56→  legitimately have 0 tests)
    57→
    58→## 20260220 - Remove classify_package and PackageKind
    59→
    60→### Context
    61→
    62→`classify_package()` hardcoded directory conventions (`apps/`, `libs/`,
    63→`tools/`, `*/tests`) specific to one workspace layout. tspec shouldn't
    64→impose layout opinions — cargo metadata already provides `has_binary`,
    65→and the only real behavioral need is excluding build tools (tspec/xt/xtask)
    66→from batch operations.
    67→
    68→### Changes
    69→
    70→**`src/workspace.rs`:**
    71→- Removed `PackageKind` enum (5 variants) and `classify_package()` fn
    72→- Removed `test_members()` method and `is_build_tool` field
    73→- `PackageMember` now has only `name`, `path`, `has_binary`
    74→- `buildable_members()` returns all members (no exclusions)
    75→- `runnable_members()` returns all members with binaries
    76→- Replaced 11 classify tests with 1 `discover_works` test
    77→
    78→**`src/all.rs`:**
    79→- Removed `PackageKind`, `PermissionsExt` imports
    80→- Removed `PackageKind::Test` skip and entire 100-line
    81→  test-binary-discovery block from `test_all()`
    82→- `test_all()` now just iterates `buildable_members()` + `test_package()`
    83→
    84→**`tests/integration_test.rs`:**
    85→- Removed `#[ignore]` from all 16 integration tests — they run
    86→  by default now since they're fast and anyone running `tspec test`
    87→  has the full toolchain
    88→
    89→### Rationale for removing is_build_tool
    90→
    91→The `xt`/`xtask` exclusions were vestigial from the rlibc-x workspace.
    92→Excluding `tspec` from its own `tspec test` was counterproductive —
    93→`tspec test` only showed tspec-build in the summary. No workspace
    94→layout opinions should be baked into tspec.
    95→
    96→### Behavioral changes
    97→
    98→- `tspec test` (all-packages mode) now includes all workspace members
    99→- `runnable_members()` returns any member with a binary (no exclusions)
   100→- Integration tests run by default (no `-- --ignored` needed)
   101→
   102→## 20260219 - Test infrastructure: fixture workspaces and test args
   103→
   104→### Context
   105→
   106→tspec has 255 unit tests but zero integration tests that actually build real packages.
   107→The top todo item is "Add a permanent test workspace for integration testing."
   108→
   109→Three project structures need test coverage:
   110→1. **POP** — Plain Old Package (single Cargo.toml, no workspace)
   111→2. **POP+WS** — POP with embedded workspace (like tspec itself)
   112→3. **POWS** — Pure Old Workspace (workspace root, no package)
   113→
   114→### Plan
   115→
   116→**dev1:** Add test selection flags to `tspec test`:
   117→- `--test <TARGET>` (repeatable) — select specific test targets
   118→- `--names/-n <NAME>...` — filter tests by name (OR-matched substrings via test harness)
   119→- `--list/-l` — list test targets and functions (grouped with counts)
   120→- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing args to test binary (--ignored, --exact, etc.)
   121→All flow through `CargoFlags.extra_args` — no new parameters to `test_package()`/`run_cargo()`.
   122→
   123→**dev2:** POP fixture in `tests/fixtures/pop/` with copy-to-tmpdir helper and `#[ignore]` integration tests.
   124→
   125→**dev3:** POP+WS fixture in `tests/fixtures/pop-ws/` with workspace discovery and cross-package tests.
   126→
   127→**dev4:** POWS fixture in `tests/fixtures/pows/` with all-packages mode tests.
   128→
   129→### Status (20260220)
   130→
   131→**Branch:** `feat-test-fixtures` off `main` (at `a98ebad`)
   132→
   133→**Completed:**
   134→- dev0: `534ce4b` — marker commit, documented -dev0 convention in CLAUDE.md + README.md
   135→- dev1: `28baac3` — test selection flags (--test, --names, --list, --target-names, trailing args)
   136→  - Also added: `extra_args: Vec<String>` to `CargoFlags` in types.rs
   137→  - Also added: workspace-mode --test auto-filters to packages that have the target
   138→  - 268 unit tests, all passing. Installed and verified.
   139→- dev2: `f85a1cb` — POP fixture + integration tests
   140→  - Created `tests/fixtures/pop/` with Cargo.toml, src/main.rs, tspec.ts.toml
   141→  - Added `exclude = ["tests/fixtures"]` to root Cargo.toml `[workspace]`
   142→  - Created `tests/fixture.rs` helper: `copy_fixture(name) -> (TempDir, PathBuf)`
   143→  - Created `tests/integration_test.rs` with 4 `#[ignore]` tests
   144→  - Bug fix: `find_project_root()` now respects workspace `exclude` list
   145→  - Added `is_excluded_from_workspace()` function + 4 unit tests
   146→- dev3: `5c24d4b` — POP+WS fixture
   147→  - Created `tests/fixtures/pop-ws/` with root binary + mylib member
   148→  - Added 6 integration tests (spec load, cargo build, tspec build root/member/all, build from member dir)
   149→  - Bug fix: `classify_package()` now uses paths relative to workspace root
   150→  - Added unit test for path-under-tests-dir classification
   151→- dev4: POWS fixture
   152→  - Created `tests/fixtures/pows/` with workspace-only root (no [package]), pows-app binary, mylib library
   153→  - Added 6 integration tests (cargo build, tspec build all/member/from-member-dir, tspec test all, dot-resolves-to-all)
   154→  - Fix: directory name must match package name for `find_package_dir()` resolution (pows-app/ not app/)
   155→  - All 16 integration tests passing, 268 unit tests passing
   156→
   157→**Remaining:** release (v0.15.0)
   158→
   159→### Future: --manifest-path / --path flag
   160→
   161→Add a way to run tspec against a project without cd'ing into it. Cargo uses `--manifest-path <path/to/Cargo.toml>` (not `--path` — unclear why). We could follow cargo's convention (`--manifest-path`) or use `--path <dir>` for convenience. Would simplify integration tests and general usability. Not blocking dev2-dev4.
   162→
   163→### Documentation TODO
   164→
   165→Where and how to document the test fixtures (decide before release):

   167→- `tests/README.md` — purpose of the tests directory, overview of unit vs integration tests
   168→- `tests/fixtures/README.md` — purpose of fixtures, how copy_fixture() works
```

```json
  "old_string": "## 20260219 - Test infrastructure: fixture workspaces and test args",
  "new_string": "## 20260223 - Add --manifest-path flag with --mp alias\n\n### Problem\n\ntspec currently requires `cd`ing into a project to operate on it.\nAdding `--manifest-path` (with `visible_alias = \"mp\"`) lets users run\n`tspec build --mp /path/to/project` from anywhere, matching cargo's\nown `--manifest-path` convention but with a shorter alias.\n\n### Approach: thread project_root through internal APIs\n\nThe core insight: `project_root` is already computed in `main.rs` and\npassed to every command's `execute()`. But three helpers re-derive it\nfrom cwd:\n\n- `resolve_package_arg()` calls `find_project_root()` internally\n- `current_package_name()` uses `std::env::current_dir()`\n- `WorkspaceInfo::discover()` runs `cargo metadata` from cwd\n\nFix: refactor these three to accept `project_root` as a parameter,\nthen wire `--manifest-path` into `main.rs` to override\n`find_project_root()`.\n\n### Plan (multi-step)\n\n- **dev1:** Refactor `resolve_package_arg()`, `current_package_name()`,\n  and `WorkspaceInfo::discover()` to accept `project_root`. Update all\n  callers. No behavior change.\n- **dev2:** Add `--manifest-path` / `--mp` global flag to CLI.\n  Add `resolve_manifest_path()` to `find_paths.rs`. Wire into\n  `main.rs` to override `find_project_root()`.\n\n### Status\n\nIn progress.\n\n## 20260219 - Test infrastructure: fixture workspaces and test args"
}
```

```
 - Integration tests run by default (no `-- --ignored` needed)
 
+## 20260223 - Add --manifest-path flag with --mp alias
+
+### Problem
+
+tspec currently requires `cd`ing into a project to operate on it.
+Adding `--manifest-path` (with `visible_alias = "mp"`) lets users run
+`tspec build --mp /path/to/project` from anywhere, matching cargo's
+own `--manifest-path` convention but with a shorter alias.
+
+### Approach: thread project_root through internal APIs
+
+The core insight: `project_root` is already computed in `main.rs` and
+passed to every command's `execute()`. But three helpers re-derive it
+from cwd:
+
+- `resolve_package_arg()` calls `find_project_root()` internally
+- `current_package_name()` uses `std::env::current_dir()`
+- `WorkspaceInfo::discover()` runs `cargo metadata` from cwd
+
+Fix: refactor these three to accept `project_root` as a parameter,
+then wire `--manifest-path` into `main.rs` to override
+`find_project_root()`.
+
+### Plan (multi-step)
+
+- **dev1:** Refactor `resolve_package_arg()`, `current_package_name()`,
+  and `WorkspaceInfo::discover()` to accept `project_root`. Update all
+  callers. No behavior change.
+- **dev2:** Add `--manifest-path` / `--mp` global flag to CLI.
+  Add `resolve_manifest_path()` to `find_paths.rs`. Wire into
+  `main.rs` to override `find_project_root()`.
+
+### Status
+
+In progress.
+
```

```
     1→//! `tspec ts show` - Show tspec contents
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     7→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
     8→
     9→use super::list::find_tspec_files;
    10→
    11→/// Show a tspec file's contents
    12→pub fn show_tspec(
    13→    project_root: &Path,
    14→    package: Option<&str>,
    15→    all: bool,
    16→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    17→) -> Result<()> {
    18→    let workspace = project_root;
    19→
    20→    // Check if we're in a package directory
    21→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
    22→    let in_package_dir = get_package_name(&cwd).is_ok();
    23→
    24→    // Resolve: --all > -p PKG > cwd > all
    25→    let show_all = all || (package.is_none() && !in_package_dir);
    26→
    27→    if let Some(name) = package {
    28→        // Explicit package specified
    29→        let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, Some(name))?;
    30→        show_package_tspecs(&package_dir, name, tspec)?;
    31→    } else if show_all {
    32→        // Show all packages
    33→        let info = crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    34→        for (i, member) in info.members.iter().enumerate() {
    35→            if i > 0 {
    36→                println!();
    37→            }
    38→            println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
    39→            show_package_tspecs(&member.path, &member.name, tspec)?;
    40→        }
    41→    } else {
    42→        // In a package directory
    43→        let pkg_name = get_package_name(&cwd)?;
    44→        show_package_tspecs(&cwd, &pkg_name, tspec)?;
    45→    }
    46→
    47→    Ok(())
    48→}
    49→
    50→/// Show tspecs for a single package
    51→fn show_package_tspecs(
    52→    package_dir: &std::path::Path,
    53→    pkg_name: &str,
    54→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    55→) -> Result<()> {
    56→    match tspec {
    57→        Some(name) => {
    58→            // Explicit tspec - show just that one
    59→            let path = find_tspec(package_dir, Some(name))?;
    60→            match path {
    61→                Some(p) => print_tspec_content(&p)?,
    62→                None => anyhow::bail!("tspec '{}' not found for package '{}'", name, pkg_name),
    63→            }
    64→        }
    65→        None => {
    66→            // No tspec specified - show all tspec files
    67→            let tspecs = find_tspec_files(package_dir)?;
    68→            if tspecs.is_empty() {
    69→                println!("No *{} files found for {}", TSPEC_SUFFIX, pkg_name);
    70→            } else {
    71→                for (i, name) in tspecs.iter().enumerate() {
    72→                    if i > 0 {
    73→                        println!();
    74→                    }
    75→                    print_tspec_content(&package_dir.join(name))?;
    76→                }
    77→            }
    78→        }
    79→    }
    80→    Ok(())
    81→}
    82→
    83→/// Print a single tspec file with header
    84→fn print_tspec_content(path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    85→    let filename = path
    86→        .file_name()
    87→        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy())
    88→        .unwrap_or_default();
    89→    println!("====== {} ======", filename);
    90→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)?;
    91→    print!("{}", content);
    92→    if !content.ends_with('\n') {
    93→        println!();
    94→    }
    95→    Ok(())
    96→}
    97→
```

```
     1→//! `tspec ts list` - List tspec files
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     7→use crate::find_paths::{get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
     8→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
     9→
    10→/// List all tspec files in workspace or for a specific package
    11→pub fn list_tspecs(project_root: &Path, package: Option<&str>, all: bool) -> Result<()> {
    12→    let workspace = project_root;
    13→
    14→    // Check if we're in a package directory (has Cargo.toml with [package], not just workspace)
    15→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
    16→    let in_package_dir = get_package_name(&cwd).is_ok();
    17→
    18→    // Resolve: --all > -p PKG > cwd > all
    19→    let list_all = all || (package.is_none() && !in_package_dir);
    20→
    21→    if let Some(name) = package {
    22→        // Explicit package specified
    23→        let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, Some(name))?;
    24→        let tspecs = find_tspec_files(&package_dir)?;
    25→        print_package_tspecs(name, &package_dir, &tspecs);
    26→    } else if list_all {
    27→        // List all packages
    28→        let info = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    29→        let mut found_any = false;
    30→
    31→        for member in &info.members {
    32→            let tspecs = find_tspec_files(&member.path)?;
    33→            if !tspecs.is_empty() {
    34→                print_package_tspecs(&member.name, &member.path, &tspecs);
    35→                found_any = true;
    36→            }
    37→        }
    38→
    39→        if !found_any {
    40→            println!("No *{} files found in workspace", TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    41→        }
    42→    } else {
    43→        // In a package directory, list just this package
    44→        let pkg_name = get_package_name(&cwd)?;
    45→        let tspecs = find_tspec_files(&cwd)?;
    46→        print_package_tspecs(&pkg_name, &cwd, &tspecs);
    47→    }
    48→
    49→    Ok(())
    50→}
    51→
    52→/// Find all tspec files (files ending with TSPEC_SUFFIX) in a directory
    53→pub(crate) fn find_tspec_files(dir: &Path) -> Result<Vec<String>> {
    54→    let mut files = Vec::new();
    55→
    56→    if let Ok(entries) = std::fs::read_dir(dir) {
    57→        for entry in entries.filter_map(|e| e.ok()) {
    58→            let name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
    59→            if name.ends_with(TSPEC_SUFFIX) {
    60→                files.push(name);
    61→            }
    62→        }
    63→    }
    64→
    65→    files.sort();
    66→    Ok(files)
    67→}
    68→
    69→/// Print tspec files for a package
    70→fn print_package_tspecs(package_name: &str, package_dir: &Path, tspecs: &[String]) {
    71→    println!("{}:", package_name);
    72→    for tspec in tspecs {
    73→        let path = package_dir.join(tspec);
    74→        let size = std::fs::metadata(&path)
    75→            .map(|m| format_size(m.len()))
    76→            .unwrap_or_else(|_| "?".to_string());
    77→        println!("  {} ({})", tspec, size);
    78→    }
    79→}
    80→
    81→/// Format file size in human-readable form
    82→pub(crate) fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
    83→    if bytes < 1024 {
    84→        format!("{} B", bytes)
    85→    } else {
    86→        format!("{:.1} KB", bytes as f64 / 1024.0)
    87→    }
    88→}
    89→
    90→#[cfg(test)]
    91→mod tests {

    99→        assert_eq!(format_size(1023), "1023 B");

   101→

   107→
```

```
    11→
    12→/// Show hash of a tspec file
    13→pub fn hash_tspec(
    14→    project_root: &Path,
    15→    package: Option<&str>,
    16→    all: bool,
    17→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    18→) -> Result<()> {
    19→    let workspace = project_root;
    20→
    21→    // Check if we're in a package directory
    22→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
    23→    let in_package_dir = get_package_name(&cwd).is_ok();
    24→

    26→    let hash_all = all || (package.is_none() && !in_package_dir);
    27→
    28→    if let Some(name) = package {
    29→        // Explicit package specified
    30→        let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, Some(name))?;
    31→        hash_package_tspecs(&package_dir, name, tspec)?;
    32→    } else if hash_all {
    33→        // Hash all packages
    34→        let info = crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    35→        for member in &info.members {
    36→            hash_package_tspecs(&member.path, &member.name, tspec)?;

    46→
    47→/// Hash tspecs for a single package
    48→fn hash_package_tspecs(
    49→    package_dir: &std::path::Path,

    52→) -> Result<()> {
    53→    match tspec {

    76→
    77→/// Print hash of a single tspec file
    78→fn print_tspec_hash(path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    79→    let filename = path
    80→        .file_name()
    81→        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy())
    82→        .unwrap_or_default();
    83→    let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    84→    let hash = hash_spec(&spec)?;
    85→    println!("{}: {}", filename, hash);
    86→    Ok(())
    87→}
    88→
```

```
     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::{Args, Subcommand};
     3→use std::path::Path;

     5→
     6→use super::Execute;
```
