     9→
    10→/// Manage translation specs
    11→#[derive(Args)]
    12→pub struct TsCmd {
    13→    #[command(subcommand)]
    14→    command: TsCommands,
    15→}
    16→
    17→#[derive(Subcommand)]
    18→pub enum TsCommands {
    19→    /// List tspec files in workspace or for a specific package
    20→    List {
    21→        /// Package to list specs for (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    22→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    23→        package: Option<String>,
    24→        /// List all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    25→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    26→        all: bool,
    27→    },
    28→    /// Show a tspec's contents
    29→    Show {
    30→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    31→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    32→        package: Option<String>,
    33→        /// Show all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    34→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    35→        all: bool,
    36→        /// Tspec name (defaults to all tspec files)
    37→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    38→        tspec: Option<String>,
    39→    },
    40→    /// Show the content hash of a tspec
    41→    Hash {
    42→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    43→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    44→        package: Option<String>,
    45→        /// Hash all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    46→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    47→        all: bool,
    48→        /// Tspec name (defaults to package's tspec file)
    49→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    50→        tspec: Option<String>,
    51→    },
    52→    /// Create a new tspec file
    53→    New {
    54→        /// Name for the new tspec (default: "tspec")
    55→        #[arg(default_value = "tspec")]
    56→        name: String,
    57→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    58→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    59→        package: Option<String>,
    60→        /// Copy from existing tspec (package/spec or just spec name in same package)
    61→        #[arg(short = 'f', long = "from")]
    62→        from: Option<String>,
    63→    },
    64→    /// Set a field in a tspec (scalar value or replace entire array)
    65→    ///
    66→    /// For scalars: tspec ts set key value
    67→    /// For arrays: tspec ts set key val1 val2 ...
    68→    Set {
    69→        /// Field key (e.g., "cargo.profile", "linker.args")
    70→        key: String,
    71→        /// Value(s). For scalars, one value. For arrays, each arg is an element.
    72→        #[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
    73→        value: Vec<String>,
    74→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    75→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    76→        package: Option<String>,
    77→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    78→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    79→        tspec: Option<String>,
    80→    },
    81→    /// Remove a field from a tspec (preserves comments)
    82→    Unset {
    83→        /// Key to remove (e.g., "cargo.profile", "panic", "linker.args")
    84→        key: String,
    85→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    86→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    87→        package: Option<String>,
    88→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    89→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    90→        tspec: Option<String>,
    91→    },
    92→    /// Add items to an array field (append by default, or insert at position)
    93→    Add {
    94→        /// Field key (must be an array field, e.g., "linker.args")
    95→        key: String,
    96→        /// Items to add
    97→        #[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
    98→        value: Vec<String>,
    99→        /// Insert at this index instead of appending
   100→        #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   101→        index: Option<usize>,
   102→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   103→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   104→        package: Option<String>,
   105→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   106→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   107→        tspec: Option<String>,
   108→    },
   109→    /// Remove items from an array field (by value or by index)
   110→    Remove {
   111→        /// Field key (must be an array field, e.g., "linker.args")
   112→        key: String,
   113→        /// Items to remove by value (not used with --index)
   114→        #[arg(allow_hyphen_values = true)]
   115→        value: Vec<String>,
   116→        /// Remove item at this index instead of by value
   117→        #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   118→        index: Option<usize>,
   119→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   120→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   121→        package: Option<String>,
   122→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   123→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   124→        tspec: Option<String>,
   125→    },
   126→    /// Create a versioned backup of a tspec
   127→    Backup {
   128→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   129→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   130→        package: Option<String>,
   131→        /// Tspec to backup (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   132→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   133→        tspec: Option<String>,
   134→    },
   135→    /// Restore a tspec from a versioned backup
   136→    Restore {
   137→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   138→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   139→        package: Option<String>,
   140→        /// Backup tspec to restore from (required, e.g., "t2-001-abcd1234.ts.toml")
   141→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   142→        tspec: String,
   143→    },
   144→}
   145→
   146→impl Execute for TsCmd {
   147→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, _flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   148→        match &self.command {
   149→            TsCommands::List { package, all } => {
   150→                ts_cmd::list_tspecs(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all)?;
   151→            }
   152→            TsCommands::Show {
   153→                package,
   154→                all,
   155→                tspec,
   156→            } => {
   157→                ts_cmd::show_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   158→            }
   159→            TsCommands::Hash {
   160→                package,
   161→                all,
   162→                tspec,
   163→            } => {
   164→                ts_cmd::hash_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   165→            }
   166→            TsCommands::New {
   167→                name,
   168→                package,
   169→                from,
   170→            } => {
   171→                ts_cmd::new_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), name, from.as_deref())?;
   172→            }
   173→            TsCommands::Set {
   174→                key,
   175→                value,
   176→                package,
   177→                tspec,
   178→            } => {
   179→                ts_cmd::set_value(
   180→                    project_root,
   181→                    package.as_deref(),
   182→                    key,
   183→                    value,
   184→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   185→                )?;
   186→            }
   187→            TsCommands::Unset {
   188→                key,
   189→                package,
   190→                tspec,
   191→            } => {
   192→                ts_cmd::unset_value(project_root, package.as_deref(), key, tspec.as_deref())?;
   193→            }
   194→            TsCommands::Add {
   195→                key,
   196→                value,
   197→                index,
   198→                package,
   199→                tspec,
   200→            } => {
   201→                ts_cmd::add_value(
   202→                    project_root,
   203→                    package.as_deref(),
   204→                    key,
   205→                    value,
   206→                    *index,
   207→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   208→                )?;
   209→            }
   210→            TsCommands::Remove {
   211→                key,
   212→                value,
   213→                index,
   214→                package,
   215→                tspec,
   216→            } => {
   217→                ts_cmd::remove_value(
   218→                    project_root,
   219→                    package.as_deref(),
   220→                    key,
   221→                    value,
   222→                    *index,
   223→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   224→                )?;
   225→            }
   226→            TsCommands::Backup { package, tspec } => {
   227→                ts_cmd::backup_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec.as_deref())?;
   228→            }
   229→            TsCommands::Restore { package, tspec } => {
   230→                ts_cmd::restore_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec)?;
   231→            }
   232→        }
   233→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   234→    }
   235→}
   236→

```
     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::Execute;
     7→use crate::find_paths::get_package_name;
     8→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
     9→
    10→/// Install a package from a local path
    11→#[derive(Args)]
    12→pub struct InstallCmd {
    13→    /// Path to package (relative or absolute)
    14→    #[arg(long)]
    15→    pub path: PathBuf,
    16→    /// Force reinstall even if already installed
    17→    #[arg(short, long)]
    18→    pub force: bool,
    19→}
    20→
    21→impl Execute for InstallCmd {
    22→    fn execute(&self, _project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    23→        let resolved = self
    24→            .path
    25→            .canonicalize()
    26→            .with_context(|| format!("path not found: {}", self.path.display()))?;
    27→
    28→        let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    29→        cmd.arg("install").arg("--path").arg(&resolved);
    30→
    31→        // Pass package name if we can determine it (needed for workspaces)
    32→        if let Ok(name) = get_package_name(&resolved) {
    33→            cmd.arg(&name);
    34→        }
    35→
    36→        if self.force {
    37→            cmd.arg("--force");
    38→        }
    39→
    40→        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
    41→
    42→        let status = cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo install")?;
    43→        if !status.success() {
    44→            bail!("cargo install failed");
    45→        }
    46→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    47→    }
    48→}
    49→
```

  "new_string": "/// Resolve a `-p` argument (path or name) to the actual cargo package name.\n/// Returns Some(name) for a package, None if it resolves to a workspace root\n/// with no `[package]` section (meaning \"operate on all packages\").\npub(crate) fn resolve_package_arg(pkg: &str, project_root: &Path) -> Result<Option<String>> {\n    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(project_root, pkg)?;\n    match get_package_name(&pkg_dir) {\n        Ok(name) => Ok(Some(name)),\n        Err(_) => Ok(None),\n    }\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cmd/mod.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package]).\n/// Returns None at a workspace root so that commands default to all-packages mode.\npub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {\n    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;\n    // At a workspace root, don't treat it as a single package\n    if cwd.join(\"Cargo.toml\").exists() && !is_pop(&cwd) {\n        return None;\n    }\n    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package]).\n/// Returns None at a workspace root so that commands default to all-packages mode.\n/// Returns None if cwd is outside project_root (e.g., --manifest-path used).\npub(crate) fn current_package_name(project_root: &Path) -> Option<String> {\n    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;\n    // If cwd is outside project_root, fall back to all-packages mode\n    if !cwd.starts_with(project_root) {\n        return None;\n    }\n    // At a workspace root, don't treat it as a single package\n    if cwd.join(\"Cargo.toml\").exists() && !is_pop(&cwd) {\n        return None;\n    }\n    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()\n}"
}

```
     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use glob::Pattern;
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::{Spec, profile_dir_name};
     7→
     8→/// Check if TOML content has an exact section header like `[workspace]` or `[package]`
     9→/// Only matches lines where the trimmed content equals `[section]` exactly
    10→fn has_toml_section_exact(content: &str, section: &str) -> bool {
    11→    let header = format!("[{}]", section);
    12→    content.lines().any(|line| line.trim() == header)
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Extract package name from Cargo.toml
    16→pub fn get_package_name(crate_dir: &Path) -> Result<String> {
    17→    let cargo_toml = crate_dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    18→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)
    19→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml.display()))?;
    20→
    21→    // Simple parsing - look for name = "..." in [package] section
    22→    let mut in_package = false;
    23→    for line in content.lines() {
    24→        let line = line.trim();
    25→        if line == "[package]" {
    26→            in_package = true;
    27→            continue;
    28→        }
    29→        if line.starts_with('[') {
    30→            in_package = false;
    31→            continue;
    32→        }
    33→        if in_package
    34→            && line.starts_with("name")
    35→            && let Some(eq_pos) = line.find('=')
    36→        {
    37→            let value = line[eq_pos + 1..].trim();
    38→            let value = value.trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'');
    39→            return Ok(value.to_string());
    40→        }
    41→    }
    42→    bail!("could not find package name in {}", cargo_toml.display());
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]
    46→/// For workspaces, returns the directory containing the workspace Cargo.toml
    47→/// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml
    48→/// Respects workspace `exclude` — a package inside an excluded path is treated as a POP.
    49→pub fn find_project_root() -> Result<PathBuf> {
    50→    let mut dir = std::env::current_dir()?;
    51→    let mut package_root: Option<PathBuf> = None;
    52→
    53→    loop {
    54→        let cargo_toml = dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    55→        if cargo_toml.exists() {
    56→            let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)?;
    57→            // Workspace takes precedence — unless the package we found is excluded
    58→            if has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace") {
    59→                if let Some(ref pkg_root) = package_root
    60→                    && is_excluded_from_workspace(&content, &dir, pkg_root)
    61→                {
    62→                    return Ok(pkg_root.clone());
    63→                }
    64→                return Ok(dir);
    65→            }
    66→            // Remember the first (deepest) package we find as potential POP root
    67→            if package_root.is_none() && has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package") {
    68→                package_root = Some(dir.clone());
    69→            }
    70→        }
    71→        if !dir.pop() {
    72→            // No workspace found, use the POP root if we found one
    73→            if let Some(root) = package_root {
    74→                return Ok(root);
    75→            }
    76→            bail!("could not find project root (no Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package])");
    77→        }
    78→    }
    79→}
    80→

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/find_paths.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Check if a package directory is excluded from a workspace.",
  "new_string": "/// Resolve a `--manifest-path` argument to a project root.\n/// Accepts a path to a Cargo.toml file or a directory containing one.\n/// Walks up from the resolved directory to find the workspace root,\n/// reusing the same logic as `find_project_root()`.\npub fn resolve_manifest_path(path: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf> {\n    let start_dir = if path.is_file() {\n        path.parent()\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!(\"invalid manifest path: {}\", path.display()))?\n            .to_path_buf()\n    } else if path.is_dir() {\n        path.to_path_buf()\n    } else {\n        bail!(\"path not found: {}\", path.display());\n    };\n\n    // Verify Cargo.toml exists at the starting directory\n    if !start_dir.join(\"Cargo.toml\").exists() {\n        bail!(\n            \"no Cargo.toml found at {}\",\n            start_dir.display()\n        );\n    }\n\n    // Walk up to find workspace root, same logic as find_project_root()\n    let mut dir = start_dir.clone();\n    let mut package_root: Option<PathBuf> = None;\n\n    loop {\n        let cargo_toml = dir.join(\"Cargo.toml\");\n        if cargo_toml.exists() {\n            let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)?;\n            if has_toml_section_exact(&content, \"workspace\") {\n                if let Some(ref pkg_root) = package_root\n                    && is_excluded_from_workspace(&content, &dir, pkg_root)\n                {\n                    return Ok(pkg_root.clone());\n                }\n                return Ok(dir);\n            }\n            if package_root.is_none()\n                && has_toml_section_exact(&content, \"package\")\n            {\n                package_root = Some(dir.clone());\n            }\n        }\n        if !dir.pop() {\n            if let Some(root) = package_root {\n                return Ok(root);\n            }\n            bail!(\n                \"no project root found from {}\",\n                start_dir.display()\n            );\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Check if a package directory is excluded from a workspace."
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/main.rs",
  "content": "use clap::Parser;\nuse std::process::ExitCode;\n\nuse tspec::cli::{Cli, Commands};\nuse tspec::cmd::Execute;\nuse tspec::find_paths::{find_project_root, resolve_manifest_path};\nuse tspec::types::{CargoFlags, Verbosity};\n\nfn main() -> Result<ExitCode, anyhow::Error> {\n    let cli = Cli::parse();\n    let flags = CargoFlags {\n        verbosity: Verbosity::from_count(cli.verbose),\n        jobs: cli.jobs,\n        extra_args: Vec::new(),\n    };\n\n    let project_root = match cli.manifest_path {\n        Some(ref path) => resolve_manifest_path(path)?,\n        None => find_project_root()?,\n    };\n\n    match cli.command {\n        Commands::Build(cmd) => {\n            return cmd.execute(&project_root, &flags);\n        }\n        Commands::Run(cmd) => {\n            return cmd.execute(&project_root, &flags);\n        }\n        Commands::Test(cmd) => {\n            return cmd.execute(&project_root, &flags);\n        }\n        Commands::Clean(cmd) => {\n            cmd.execute(&project_root, &flags)?;\n        }\n        Commands::Clippy(cmd) => {\n            cmd.execute(&project_root, &flags)?;\n        }\n        Commands::Fmt(cmd) => {\n            cmd.execute(&project_root, &flags)?;\n        }\n        Commands::Compare(cmd) => {\n            cmd.execute(&project_root, &flags)?;\n        }\n        Commands::Ts(cmd) => {\n            cmd.execute(&project_root, &flags)?;\n        }\n        Commands::Version(cmd) => {\n            cmd.execute(&project_root, &flags)?;\n        }\n        Commands::Install(cmd) => {\n            cmd.execute(&project_root, &flags)?;\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)\n}\n"
}

  "old_string": "/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success.\npub fn build_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    let (build_result, _) = run_cargo(CargoMode::Build, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)?;\n    Ok(build_result)\n}\n\n/// Test a package with a spec, returns raw `test result:` lines.\npub fn test_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<Vec<String>> {\n    let (_, matched_lines) = run_cargo(CargoMode::Test, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)?;\n    Ok(matched_lines)\n}\n\n/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.\n/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.\npub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    build_package(pkg_name, None, Some(\"release\"), flags)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success.\npub fn build_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n    project_root: &Path,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    let (build_result, _) =\n        run_cargo(CargoMode::Build, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, project_root, flags)?;\n    Ok(build_result)\n}\n\n/// Test a package with a spec, returns raw `test result:` lines.\npub fn test_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n    project_root: &Path,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<Vec<String>> {\n    let (_, matched_lines) =\n        run_cargo(CargoMode::Test, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, project_root, flags)?;\n    Ok(matched_lines)\n}\n\n/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.\n/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.\npub fn plain_cargo_build_release(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    project_root: &Path,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    build_package(pkg_name, None, Some(\"release\"), project_root, flags)\n}"

     9→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};

    20→/// Normalize tspec patterns for per-package matching in all-packages mode.
    21→///
    22→/// Strips directory components so shell-expanded paths from the workspace root
    23→/// don't leak into sub-packages. Filters out non-tspec shell expansions
    24→/// (e.g. `target/`, `tools/`) — only `.ts.toml` files and glob patterns are kept.
    25→///
    26→/// Returns `None` if all patterns were filtered out (likely shell expansion of a
    27→/// glob that matched non-tspec entries). The caller should warn about quoting.
    28→fn normalize_tspec_patterns(patterns: &[String]) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    29→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    30→        return Some(Vec::new());
    31→    }
    32→    let filenames: Vec<String> = patterns
    33→        .iter()
    34→        .filter_map(|p| {
    35→            let name = Path::new(p)
    36→                .file_name()
    37→                .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
    38→                .unwrap_or_else(|| p.clone());
    39→            // Keep glob patterns (unexpanded) and actual tspec files
    40→            let is_glob = name.contains('*') || name.contains('?') || name.contains('[');
    41→            let is_tspec = name.ends_with(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    42→            if is_glob || is_tspec {
    43→                Some(name)
    44→            } else {
    45→                None
    46→            }
    47→        })
    48→        .collect();
    49→    if filenames.is_empty() && !patterns.is_empty() {
    50→        None // all patterns were filtered — likely shell expansion
    51→    } else {
    52→        Some(filenames)
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Warn that shell glob expansion likely ate the tspec pattern.
    57→pub fn warn_shell_glob_expansion(patterns: &[String]) {
    58→    eprintln!(
    59→        "Warning: -t arguments ({}) don't look like tspec files.",
    60→        patterns.join(", ")
    61→    );
    62→    eprintln!("  The shell likely expanded your glob before tspec could see it.");
    63→    eprintln!("  Quote the pattern to prevent shell expansion: -t 'pattern*'");
    64→}
    65→
    66→/// Resolve tspec patterns for a workspace member.
    67→///
    68→/// Returns the matching spec paths, or an empty vec if no patterns match.
    69→/// When `patterns` is empty, returns an empty vec (caller should use default behavior).
    70→fn resolve_specs_for_member(member_path: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    71→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    72→        return Vec::new();
    73→    }
    74→    find_tspecs(member_path, patterns).unwrap_or_default()
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
    78→fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    79→    match tspec {
    80→        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
    81→        None => String::new(),
    82→    }
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
    86→pub struct OpResult {
    87→    pub name: String,
    88→    pub spec: String,
    89→    pub success: bool,
    90→    pub message: String,
    91→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    92→    pub test_counts: Option<TestResult>,
    93→}
    94→
    95→/// Build all workspace packages (excluding build tools)
    96→///
    97→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
    98→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
    99→pub fn build_all(
   100→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   101→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   102→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   103→    strip: bool,
   104→    fail_fast: bool,
   105→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   106→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   107→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   108→        Some(n) => n,
   109→        None => {
   110→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   111→            return Vec::new();
   112→        }
   113→    };
   114→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   115→
   116→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   117→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   118→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   119→            continue;
   120→        }
   121→
   122→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   123→
   124→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   125→            vec![None]
   126→        } else {
   127→            specs
   128→                .into_iter()
   129→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   130→                .collect()
   131→        };
   132→
   133→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   134→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   135→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   136→                Ok(build_result) => {
   137→                    if strip
   138→                        && member.has_binary
   139→                        && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
   140→                    {
   141→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   142→                    }
   143→                    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
   144→                    OpResult {
   145→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   146→                        spec,
   147→                        success: true,
   148→                        message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
   149→                        size,
   150→                        test_counts: None,
   151→                    }
   152→                }
   153→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   154→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   155→                    spec,
   156→                    success: false,
   157→                    message: e.to_string(),
   158→                    size: None,
   159→                    test_counts: None,
   160→                },
   161→            };
   162→
   163→            let failed = !result.success;
   164→            results.push(result);
   165→
   166→            if failed && fail_fast {
   167→                return results;
   168→            }
   169→        }
   170→    }
   171→
   172→    results
   173→}
   174→
   175→/// Run all app packages sequentially
   176→///
   177→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   178→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   179→pub fn run_all(
   180→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   181→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   182→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   183→    strip: bool,
   184→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   185→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   186→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   187→        Some(n) => n,
   188→        None => {
   189→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   190→            return Vec::new();
   191→        }
   192→    };
   193→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   194→
   195→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
   196→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   197→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   198→            continue;
   199→        }
   200→
   201→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   202→
   203→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   204→            vec![None]
   205→        } else {
   206→            specs
   207→                .into_iter()
   208→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   209→                .collect()
   210→        };
   211→
   212→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   213→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   214→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   215→                Ok(build_result) => {
   216→                    if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
   217→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   218→                    }
   219→                    match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
   220→                        Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
   221→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   222→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   223→                            success: true,
   224→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   225→                            size: None,
   226→                            test_counts: None,
   227→                        },
   228→                        Err(e) => OpResult {
   229→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   230→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   231→                            success: false,
   232→                            message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   233→                            size: None,
   234→                            test_counts: None,
   235→                        },
   236→                    }
   237→                }
   238→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   239→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   240→                    spec,
   241→                    success: false,
   242→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   243→                    size: None,
   244→                    test_counts: None,
   245→                },
   246→            };
   247→
   248→            results.push(result);
   249→        }
   250→    }
   251→
   252→    results
   253→}

   255→/// Test all workspace packages
   256→///
   257→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   258→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   259→pub fn test_all(
   260→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   261→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   262→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   263→    fail_fast: bool,
   264→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   265→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   266→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   267→        Some(n) => n,
   268→        None => {
   269→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   270→            return Vec::new();
   271→        }
   272→    };
   273→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   274→
   275→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   276→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   277→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   278→            continue;
   279→        }
   280→
   281→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   282→
   283→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   284→            vec![None]
   285→        } else {
   286→            specs
   287→                .into_iter()
   288→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   289→                .collect()
   290→        };
   291→
   292→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   293→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   294→            let result = match test_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   295→                Ok(result_lines) => {
   296→                    let counts = parse_test_results(&result_lines);
   297→                    OpResult {
   298→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   299→                        spec,
   300→                        success: true,
   301→                        message: "ok".to_string(),
   302→                        size: None,
   303→                        test_counts: Some(counts),
   304→                    }
   305→                }
   306→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   307→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   308→                    spec,
   309→                    success: false,
   310→                    message: e.to_string(),
   311→                    size: None,
   312→                    test_counts: None,
   313→                },
   314→            };
   315→
   316→            let failed = !result.success;
   317→            results.push(result);
   318→
   319→            if failed && fail_fast {
   320→                return results;
   321→            }
   322→        }
   323→    }
   324→
   325→    results
   326→}

   328→/// A row for the summary table: package name, spec, and pre-formatted detail string.
   329→struct SummaryRow {
   330→    name: String,
   331→    spec: String,
   332→    detail: String,
   333→}
   334→
   335→/// Print a summary table with consistent formatting across all operations.
   336→///
   337→/// - `ws_name` — workspace/package name for the header
   338→/// - `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   339→/// - `detail_header` — column header for the detail column (e.g. "Status", "Status    Size", "Exit")
   340→/// - `rows` — pre-formatted rows
   341→/// - `footer` — footer line (e.g. "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed")
   342→fn print_summary_table(
   343→    ws_name: &str,
   344→    cmd: &str,
   345→    detail_header: &str,
   346→    rows: &[SummaryRow],
   347→    footer: &str,
   348→) {
   349→    let max_name_len = rows.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(7).max(7);
   350→    let has_spec = rows.iter().any(|r| !r.spec.is_empty());
   351→    let max_spec_len = if has_spec {
   352→        rows.iter().map(|r| r.spec.len()).max().unwrap_or(4).max(4)
   353→    } else {
   354→        0
   355→    };
   356→
   357→    println!();
   358→    print_header!(format!("{ws_name} {cmd} SUMMARY"));
   359→    if has_spec {
   360→        println!(
   361→            "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {detail_header}",
   362→            "Package",
   363→            "Spec",
   364→            nw = max_name_len,
   365→            sw = max_spec_len
   366→        );
   367→    } else {
   368→        println!(
   369→            "  {:width$}  {detail_header}",
   370→            "Package",
   371→            width = max_name_len
   372→        );
   373→    }
   374→
   375→    for row in rows {
   376→        if has_spec {
   377→            println!(
   378→                "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {}",
   379→                row.name,
   380→                row.spec,
   381→                row.detail,
   382→                nw = max_name_len,
   383→                sw = max_spec_len
   384→            );
   385→        } else {
   386→            println!(
   387→                "  {:width$}  {}",
   388→                row.name,
   389→                row.detail,
   390→                width = max_name_len
   391→            );
   392→        }
   393→    }
   394→
   395→    println!();
   396→    if !footer.is_empty() {
   397→        println!("  {footer}");
   398→    }
   399→    print_hline!();
   400→    println!();
   401→}
   402→
   403→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   404→pub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   405→    let mut pkg_passed = 0;
   406→    let mut pkg_failed = 0;
   407→    let mut total = TestResult::default();
   408→
   409→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   410→        .iter()
   411→        .map(|r| {
   412→            let detail = if r.success {
   413→                pkg_passed += 1;
   414→                if let Some(counts) = &r.test_counts {
   415→                    total.merge(counts);
   416→                    format!("[PASS]  {} passed", counts.passed)
   417→                } else {
   418→                    "[PASS]".to_string()
   419→                }
   420→            } else {
   421→                pkg_failed += 1;
   422→                if let Some(counts) = &r.test_counts {
   423→                    total.merge(counts);
   424→                    format!("[FAIL]  {} failed", counts.failed)
   425→                } else {
   426→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   427→                }
   428→            };
   429→            SummaryRow {
   430→                name: r.name.clone(),
   431→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   432→                detail,
   433→            }
   434→        })
   435→        .collect();
   436→
   437→    let pkg_count = pkg_passed + pkg_failed;
   438→    let footer = format!(
   439→        "Test: {} packages, {} passed, {} failed",
   440→        pkg_count, total.passed, total.failed
   441→    );
   442→
   443→    print_summary_table(name, "TEST", "Status", &rows, &footer);
   444→
   445→    if pkg_failed > 0 {
   446→        ExitCode::from(1)
   447→    } else {
   448→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   449→    }
   450→}
   451→
   452→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   453→pub fn print_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   454→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   455→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   456→
   457→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   458→        .iter()
   459→        .map(|r| {
   460→            let status = if r.success {
   461→                ok_count += 1;
   462→                "[ OK ]"
   463→            } else {
   464→                failed_count += 1;
   465→                "[FAIL]"
   466→            };
   467→            let size_str = r.size.map(format_size).unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   468→            SummaryRow {
   469→                name: r.name.clone(),
   470→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   471→                detail: format!("{status}  {size_str:>6}"),
   472→            }
   473→        })
   474→        .collect();
   475→
   476→    print_summary_table(
   477→        name,
   478→        "BUILD",
   479→        "Status    Size",
   480→        &rows,
   481→        &format!("Build: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   482→    );
   483→
   484→    if failed_count > 0 {
   485→        ExitCode::from(1)
   486→    } else {
   487→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   488→    }
   489→}
   490→
   491→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   492→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   493→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   494→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   495→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   496→    } else {
   497→        format!("{}", bytes)
   498→    }
   499→}

   501→/// Result of a compare operation on a single package
   502→pub struct CompareResult {
   503→    pub op: OpResult,
   504→    pub specs: Vec<SpecResult>,
   505→}
   506→
   507→/// Compare all workspace packages that have binaries
   508→///
   509→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package discovers its own specs via default glob.
   510→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   511→pub fn compare_all(
   512→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   513→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   514→    fail_fast: bool,
   515→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   516→) -> Vec<CompareResult> {
   517→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   518→        Some(n) => n,
   519→        None => {
   520→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   521→            return Vec::new();
   522→        }
   523→    };
   524→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   525→
   526→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   527→        if !member.has_binary {
   528→            continue;
   529→        }
   530→
   531→        let spec_paths = if normalized.is_empty() {
   532→            find_tspecs(&member.path, &[]).unwrap_or_default()
   533→        } else {
   534→            let resolved = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   535→            if resolved.is_empty() {
   536→                continue;
   537→            }
   538→            resolved
   539→        };
   540→
   541→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   542→
   543→        let (op, specs) = match compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths, flags) {
   544→            Ok(spec_results) => (
   545→                OpResult {
   546→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   547→                    spec: String::new(),
   548→                    success: true,
   549→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   550→                    size: None,
   551→                    test_counts: None,
   552→                },
   553→                spec_results,
   554→            ),
   555→            Err(e) => (
   556→                OpResult {
   557→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   558→                    spec: String::new(),
   559→                    success: false,
   560→                    message: e.to_string(),
   561→                    size: None,
   562→                    test_counts: None,
   563→                },
   564→                Vec::new(),
   565→            ),
   566→        };
   567→
   568→        let failed = !op.success;
   569→        results.push(CompareResult { op, specs });
   570→
   571→        if failed && fail_fast {
   572→            break;
   573→        }
   574→    }
   575→
   576→    results
   577→}
   578→
   579→/// Print a summary for compare operations
   580→///
   581→/// With a single package, just prints its comparison table.
   582→/// With multiple packages, reprints all per-package tables then an overall OK/FAIL summary.
   583→pub fn print_compare_summary(name: &str, results: &[CompareResult]) -> ExitCode {
   584→    let has_failure = results.iter().any(|r| !r.op.success);
   585→
   586→    // Reprint per-package comparison tables together
   587→    for result in results {
   588→        if !result.specs.is_empty() {
   589→            print_comparison(&result.op.name, &result.specs);
   590→        }
   591→    }
   592→
   593→    // Only show overall COMPARE SUMMARY when there are multiple packages
   594→    if results.len() > 1 {
   595→        let mut ok_count = 0;
   596→        let mut failed_count = 0;
   597→
   598→        let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   599→            .iter()
   600→            .map(|r| {
   601→                let detail = if r.op.success {
   602→                    ok_count += 1;
   603→                    "[ OK ]".to_string()
   604→                } else {
   605→                    failed_count += 1;
   606→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   607→                };
   608→                SummaryRow {
   609→                    name: r.op.name.clone(),
   610→                    spec: r.op.spec.clone(),
   611→                    detail,
   612→                }
   613→            })
   614→            .collect();
   615→
   616→        print_summary_table(
   617→            name,
   618→            "COMPARE",
   619→            "Status",
   620→            &rows,
   621→            &format!("Compare: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   622→        );
   623→    }
   624→
   625→    if has_failure {
   626→        ExitCode::from(1)
   627→    } else {
   628→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   629→    }
   630→}
   631→
   632→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   633→pub fn print_run_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   634→    let mut error_count = 0;
   635→
   636→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   637→        .iter()
   638→        .map(|r| {
   639→            let detail = if r.success {
   640→                let code = r.message.strip_prefix("exit code: ").unwrap_or(&r.message);
   641→                format!("{code:>4}")
   642→            } else {
   643→                error_count += 1;
   644→                format!("ERROR: {}", r.message)
   645→            };
   646→            SummaryRow {
   647→                name: r.name.clone(),
   648→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   649→                detail,
   650→            }
   651→        })
   652→        .collect();
   653→
   654→    let footer = if error_count > 0 {
   655→        format!("Run: {error_count} error(s)")
   656→    } else {
   657→        String::new()
   658→    };
   659→
   660→    print_summary_table(name, "RUN", "Exit", &rows, &footer);
   661→
   662→    if error_count > 0 {
   663→        ExitCode::from(1)
   664→    } else {
   665→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   666→    }
   667→}
   668→
   669→#[cfg(test)]
   670→mod tests {
   671→    use super::*;
   672→    use std::process::ExitCode;
   673→
   674→    fn make_op(name: &str, success: bool, counts: Option<TestResult>) -> OpResult {
   675→        OpResult {
   676→            name: name.to_string(),
   677→            spec: String::new(),
   678→            success,
   679→            message: if success {
   680→                "ok".to_string()
   681→            } else {
   682→                "failed".to_string()
   683→            },
   684→            size: None,
   685→            test_counts: counts,
   686→        }
   687→    }
   688→
   689→    #[test]
   690→    fn test_summary_all_pass() {
   691→        let results = vec![
   692→            make_op(
   693→                "pkg-a",
   694→                true,
   695→                Some(TestResult {
   696→                    passed: 10,
   697→                    failed: 0,
   698→                    ignored: 0,
   699→                    filtered: 0,
   700→                }),
   701→            ),
   702→            make_op(
   703→                "pkg-b",
   704→                true,
   705→                Some(TestResult {
   706→                    passed: 5,
   707→                    failed: 0,
   708→                    ignored: 0,
   709→                    filtered: 0,
   710→                }),
   711→            ),
   712→        ];
   713→        let code = print_test_summary("test", &results);
   714→        assert_eq!(code, ExitCode::SUCCESS);
   715→    }
   716→
   717→    #[test]
   718→    fn test_summary_with_failure() {
   719→        let results = vec![
   720→            make_op(
   721→                "pkg-a",
   722→                true,
   723→                Some(TestResult {
   724→                    passed: 10,
   725→                    failed: 0,
   726→                    ignored: 0,
   727→                    filtered: 0,
   728→                }),
   729→            ),
   730→            make_op(
   731→                "pkg-b",
   732→                false,
   733→                Some(TestResult {
   734→                    passed: 1,
   735→                    failed: 2,
   736→                    ignored: 0,
   737→                    filtered: 0,
   738→                }),
   739→            ),
   740→        ];
   741→        let code = print_test_summary("test", &results);
   742→        assert_eq!(code, ExitCode::from(1));
   743→    }
   744→
   745→    #[test]
   746→    fn test_summary_no_counts() {
   747→        // Packages without test_counts (e.g., build failure before tests ran)
   748→        let results = vec![make_op("pkg-a", false, None)];
   749→        let code = print_test_summary("test", &results);
   750→        assert_eq!(code, ExitCode::from(1));
   751→    }
   752→}

     1→use std::path::Path;
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→
     5→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
     6→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, plain_cargo_build_release};
     7→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
     8→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
     9→
    10→/// Result of building a spec
    11→pub struct SpecResult {
    12→    pub name: String,
    13→    pub size: u64,
    14→}
    15→
    16→/// Compare multiple specs for a package
    17→pub fn compare_specs(
    18→    pkg_name: &str,
    19→    spec_paths: &[impl AsRef<Path> + std::fmt::Debug],
    20→    flags: &CargoFlags,
    21→) -> Result<Vec<SpecResult>> {
    22→    println!("Comparing {} builds:\n", pkg_name);
    23→
    24→    let mut results = Vec::new();
    25→
    26→    // Always build cargo --release baseline first (unstripped + stripped)
    27→    match build_baseline(pkg_name, flags) {
    28→        Ok((size, stripped_size)) => {
    29→            results.push(SpecResult {
    30→                name: "cargo --release".to_string(),
    31→                size,
    32→            });
    33→            results.push(SpecResult {
    34→                name: "cargo --release-strip".to_string(),
    35→                size: stripped_size,
    36→            });
    37→        }
    38→        Err(_) => {
    39→            println!("    baseline build failed, skipping");
    40→        }
    41→    }
    42→    println!();
    43→
    44→    for spec_path in spec_paths {
    45→        let spec_path = spec_path.as_ref();
    46→        let name = spec_path
    47→            .file_name()
    48→            .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
    49→            .unwrap_or_else(|| spec_path.display().to_string());
    50→
    51→        let size = build_spec(pkg_name, spec_path, flags)?;
    52→        results.push(SpecResult { name, size });
    53→        println!();
    54→    }
    55→
    56→    // Sort by size (smallest first)
    57→    results.sort_by_key(|r| r.size);
    58→
    59→    Ok(results)
    60→}
    61→
    62→/// Build baseline and return (unstripped_size, stripped_size)
    63→fn build_baseline(pkg_name: &str, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<(u64, u64)> {
    64→    println!("  cargo --release:");
    65→
    66→    let build_result = plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name, flags)?;
    67→
    68→    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    69→    println!("    size: {} bytes", format_size(size));
    70→
    71→    strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    72→    let stripped_size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    73→
    74→    Ok((size, stripped_size))
    75→}
    76→
    77→fn build_spec(pkg_name: &str, spec_path: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<u64> {
    78→    let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    79→    println!(
    80→        "  {}:",
    81→        spec_path.file_name().unwrap_or_default().to_string_lossy()
    82→    );
    83→
    84→    // Build using spec settings (profile, strip, etc. are all in the spec)
    85→    let build_result = build_package(pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), None, flags)?;
    86→
    87→    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    88→    println!("    size: {} bytes", format_size(size));
    89→
    90→    Ok(size)
    91→}
    92→
    93→pub fn print_comparison(pkg_name: &str, results: &[SpecResult]) {
    94→    let largest_size = results.iter().map(|r| r.size).max().unwrap_or(0);
    95→    let max_name_len = results.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(4);
    96→
    97→    // Format percent change: show reduction with minus sign, baseline as 0.0%
    98→    let fmt_pct = |size: u64| -> String {
    99→        if largest_size == 0 {
   100→            return "   0.0%".to_string();
   101→        }
   102→        let pct = ((largest_size as f64 - size as f64) / largest_size as f64) * 100.0;
   103→        if pct > 0.0 {
   104→            format!("{:>7.1}%", -pct)
   105→        } else {
   106→            "   0.0%".to_string()
   107→        }
   108→    };
   109→
   110→    println!();
   111→    print_header!(format!("{} COMPARE SUMMARY", pkg_name));
   112→    println!(
   113→        "  {:width$}  {:>10}  {:>8}",
   114→        "Spec",
   115→        "Size",
   116→        "Change",
   117→        width = max_name_len
   118→    );
   119→    for result in results {
   120→        println!(
   121→            "  {:width$}  {:>10}  {}",
   122→            result.name,
   123→            format_size(result.size),
   124→            fmt_pct(result.size),
   125→            width = max_name_len
   126→        );
   127→    }
   128→    print_hline!();
   129→    println!();
   130→}
   131→
   132→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   133→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   134→        format!("{:.3}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   135→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   136→        format!("{:.3}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   137→    } else {
   138→        format!("{}", bytes)
   139→    }
   140→}
   141→
```

> AGENT

The issue is `let workspace = project_root;` — `workspace` is `&Path` and then `&workspace` is `&&Path`. Simplest fix: just remove the alias and use `project_root` directly. But that's a huge diff. Instead, just drop the `&` on all the `&workspace` references where clippy complains:

Actually, the cleanest fix is to remove `let workspace = project_root;` and replace `workspace` with `project_root` in this function. But that's a lot of changes in one function. Let me just do the simple approach — the `&` is unnecessary since `workspace` is already `&Path`:

> TOOL

Lines 173-355 are the ones in `run_cargo` that still use `workspace`. I need a local variable since removing it means changing many lines. Let me just not shadow it — clippy's complaint is `&workspace` when `workspace` is already `&Path`. I'll just rename it back but not have it be a borrow issue. Actually the simplest approach: keep the local alias but change it from `let workspace = project_root` (which makes workspace `&Path`) back — the original code had `let workspace = find_project_root()?` which made it `PathBuf`. Since we're now passing `&Path`, the `&workspace` is a double-borrow.

Let me just use `project_root` directly everywhere in `run_cargo`:

   310→    // Execute the command — tee stdout for test mode to capture result lines
   311→    let (status, matched_lines) = if mode == CargoMode::Test {
   312→        // Suppress "running 0 tests" noise and subsequent empty result lines.
   313→        // Cargo emits: "running 0 tests" → "" → "test result: ok. 0 passed; ..."
   314→        let mut suppressing_zero_block = false;
   315→        let tee = tee_stdout(
   316→            &mut cmd,
   317→            |line| line.starts_with("test result:"),
   318→            |line| {
   319→                if line.trim() == "running 0 tests" {
   320→                    suppressing_zero_block = true;
   321→                    return true;
   322→                }
   323→                if suppressing_zero_block {
   324→                    // Keep suppressing blank lines and the empty result line
   325→                    if line.trim().is_empty() {
   326→                        return true;
   327→                    }
   328→                    if line.starts_with("test result: ok. 0 passed; 0 failed;") {
   329→                        suppressing_zero_block = false;
   330→                        return true;
   331→                    }
   332→                    // Non-blank, non-result line — stop suppressing
   333→                    suppressing_zero_block = false;
   334→                }
   335→                false
   336→            },
   337→        )
   338→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", mode.subcommand()))?;
   339→        (tee.status, tee.matched_lines)
   340→    } else {
   341→        let s = cmd
   342→            .status()
   343→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", mode.subcommand()))?;
   344→        (s, Vec::new())
   345→    };

   362→            }
   363→            None => bail!("cargo {} failed for `{}`", mode.subcommand(), pkg_name),
   364→        }
   365→    }

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use glob::Pattern;
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::{Spec, profile_dir_name};
     7→
     8→/// Check if TOML content has an exact section header like `[workspace]` or `[package]`
     9→/// Only matches lines where the trimmed content equals `[section]` exactly
    10→fn has_toml_section_exact(content: &str, section: &str) -> bool {
    11→    let header = format!("[{}]", section);
    12→    content.lines().any(|line| line.trim() == header)
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Extract package name from Cargo.toml
    16→pub fn get_package_name(crate_dir: &Path) -> Result<String> {
    17→    let cargo_toml = crate_dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    18→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)
    19→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml.display()))?;
    20→
    21→    // Simple parsing - look for name = "..." in [package] section
    22→    let mut in_package = false;
    23→    for line in content.lines() {
    24→        let line = line.trim();
    25→        if line == "[package]" {
    26→            in_package = true;
    27→            continue;
    28→        }
    29→        if line.starts_with('[') {
    30→            in_package = false;
    31→            continue;
    32→        }
    33→        if in_package
    34→            && line.starts_with("name")
    35→            && let Some(eq_pos) = line.find('=')
    36→        {
    37→            let value = line[eq_pos + 1..].trim();
    38→            let value = value.trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'');
    39→            return Ok(value.to_string());
    40→        }
    41→    }
    42→    bail!("could not find package name in {}", cargo_toml.display());
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]
    46→/// For workspaces, returns the directory containing the workspace Cargo.toml
    47→/// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml
    48→/// Respects workspace `exclude` — a package inside an excluded path is treated as a POP.
    49→pub fn find_project_root() -> Result<PathBuf> {
    50→    let mut dir = std::env::current_dir()?;
    51→    let mut package_root: Option<PathBuf> = None;
    52→
    53→    loop {
    54→        let cargo_toml = dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    55→        if cargo_toml.exists() {
    56→            let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)?;
    57→            // Workspace takes precedence — unless the package we found is excluded
    58→            if has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace") {
    59→                if let Some(ref pkg_root) = package_root
    60→                    && is_excluded_from_workspace(&content, &dir, pkg_root)
    61→                {
    62→                    return Ok(pkg_root.clone());
    63→                }
    64→                return Ok(dir);
    65→            }
    66→            // Remember the first (deepest) package we find as potential POP root
    67→            if package_root.is_none() && has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package") {
    68→                package_root = Some(dir.clone());
    69→            }
    70→        }
    71→        if !dir.pop() {
    72→            // No workspace found, use the POP root if we found one
    73→            if let Some(root) = package_root {
    74→                return Ok(root);
    75→            }
    76→            bail!("could not find project root (no Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package])");
    77→        }
    78→    }
    79→}
    80→
```

> AGENT

Found the bug. `resolve_manifest_path` receives a relative path (`../pop`) and uses it as-is. The `dir.pop()` walk-up doesn't work on relative paths — it just strips path components instead of walking the actual filesystem. `find_project_root` avoids this because it starts from `current_dir()` (absolute).

The fix is to canonicalize the path at the start.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01HidtBAiVTYM5cBuYS3gkCB
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/find_paths.rs",
  "old_string": "pub fn resolve_manifest_path(path: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf> {\n    let start_dir = if path.is_file() {\n        path.parent()\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!(\"invalid manifest path: {}\", path.display()))?\n            .to_path_buf()\n    } else if path.is_dir() {\n        path.to_path_buf()\n    } else {\n        bail!(\"path not found: {}\", path.display());\n    };",
  "new_string": "pub fn resolve_manifest_path(path: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf> {\n    // Canonicalize so walk-up works on relative paths\n    let canon = path\n        .canonicalize()\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"path not found: {}\", path.display()))?;\n    let start_dir = if canon.is_file() {\n        canon\n            .parent()\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!(\"invalid manifest path: {}\", path.display()))?\n            .to_path_buf()\n    } else {\n        canon\n    };"
}
```

**Fixture layout:**

tests/fixtures/popws-3sp/

├── tspec.ts.toml       # workspace-level spec (optional?)

**Unit tests** (in `find_paths.rs` or a new test module): Test that `resolve_manifest_path` returns the correct workspace root when given:

**Integration tests** (in `tests/integration_tests.rs`): Test `--mp` end-to-end:
- `--mp popws-3sp` builds/tests all 3 packages
- `--mp popws-3sp -p app-a` targets just `app-a`
- `--mp popws-3sp/app-b` resolves correctly
- `--mp popws-3sp/lib-c/Cargo.toml` resolves correctly

## Test Fixtures and Integration Tests Exploration

I've thoroughly explored the test fixtures and integration tests in the tspec repository. Here's the complete analysis:

---

## 1. Directory Structure of Test Fixtures

├── mylib/

## 3. Integration Tests (`tests/integration_test.rs`)

**Key patterns:**

1. **Fixture helper module** (`fixture.rs`):
   - `copy_fixture(name: &str) -> (TempDir, PathBuf)` - copies fixture to temp dir, skipping `target/`
   - Returns tuple of `(TempDir, PathBuf)` where PathBuf is the copied project root

2. **Test naming convention**: `<fixture>_<command>_<variant>`
   - Examples: `pop_spec_loads_correctly`, `pows_tspec_build_all_from_root`

3. **Binary reference**: Uses `"tspec"` string (expects binary in PATH)

4. **Test command patterns**:
   ```rust
   Command::new("cargo").args(["build"]).current_dir(&project)
   Command::new(tspec_bin()).args(["build", "."]).current_dir(&project)
   ```

5. **Assertions**:
   - Check `output.status.success()`
   - Parse stdout with `String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout)`
   - Look for keywords like "1 failed", "[PASS]", "[FAIL]", "passed", "failed"

6. **Test categories**:

## 4. Unit Tests in `find_paths.rs`

Comprehensive unit tests in `src/find_paths.rs` (lines 412-1046):

**Test modules:**
- `get_package_name` tests (5 tests)
  - Valid package, single quotes, missing name, missing file, name in other section ignored
- `find_package_dir` tests (6 tests)
  - As path, in libs/, in apps/, libs preferred over apps, not found
- `find_tspec` tests (7 tests)
  - Explicit path, relative to crate, default exists/not exists, explicit not found, suffix fallback, no suffix fallback when has extension
- `find_tspecs` tests (8 tests)
  - Default pattern, explicit glob, explicit files, sorted and deduped, none found errors, glob matches multi-dot filenames
- `get_binary_path` tests (15 tests)
  - Simple debug/release/custom, empty spec, spec profile overrides CLI, target triple, target json, custom profiles, dev → debug mapping
- `get_binary_path` with `target_dir` tests (2 tests)
  - With target_dir and triple, without triple
- `is_excluded_from_workspace` tests (4 tests)
  - Exact match, nested match, not excluded member, no exclude list

**Key patterns:**
- Use `TempDir` for isolated test directories
- Use `fs::write()` to create test files
- Assertions with `.unwrap()` for success paths, `.is_err()` for error paths
- Extract error message context with `.to_string().contains(...)`

## 5. Key Conventions Summary

1. **Fixture structure**: Minimal TOML files, simple src/, `tspec.ts.toml` at package root, skip `target/` in copies
2. **Test naming**: snake_case, descriptive (fixture_command_variant)
3. **Fixture copying**: Always use helper function, never hardcode paths
4. **Command invocation**: Use `Command::new()` with `.args()` and `.current_dir()`
5. **Output parsing**: Convert with `.to_string_lossy()`, search for keywords
6. **Error testing**: Mark with `#[ignore]`, run separately with `-- --ignored`
7. **Unit tests**: Use `TempDir`, minimal setup, assert both success and error cases
8. **Fixture naming**: Suffix fail variants with `-fail`, workspace variants with `-ws` or `-pows`

```
     1→# Chores 6
     2→
     3→## 20260220 - Parse cargo test output for counts and filtering
     4→
     5→### Problem
     6→
     7→`tspec test` streams cargo output live but has no insight into what
     8→happened. The summary shows only `[PASS]`/`[FAIL]` per package with
     9→no test counts. Worse, 0 tests running reports as success — a filter
    10→typo silently passes. And `--test <target>` emits noisy "running 0
    11→tests" blocks for non-matching targets within a package.
    12→
    13→### Solution
    14→
    15→Tee stdout line-by-line (live output + selective capture), parse
    16→cargo's `test result:` lines for counts, and surface them:
    17→
    18→- Per-package counts in summary: `[PASS]  263 passed`
    19→- Aggregate footer: `Test: 2 packages, 279 passed, 0 failed`
    20→- Fail (exit 1) when 0 tests ran in single-package mode
    21→- Filter "running 0 tests" noise
    22→
    23→Infrastructure in a reusable `tee.rs` module so future commands
    24→(build warnings, benchmarks) can use the same pattern.
    25→
    26→### Plan
    27→
    28→- **dev1:** tee.rs + TestResult + parse_test_result_line + wire into
    29→  run_cargo for test mode. No visible behavior change.
    30→- **dev2:** Summary counts, 0-test failure, noise filtering.
    31→- **dev3:** Fail fixtures + failure-path tests.
    32→
    33→### Decision: fail fixtures for testing failure paths
    34→
    35→Considered external repos vs in-tree fixtures. In-tree is better
    36→for normal development. Fail fixtures (`pop-fail`, `pows-fail`)
    37→live in `tests/fixtures/`, workspace-excluded, with actual failing
    38→tests baked in. Integration tests using them are `#[ignore]`'d —
    39→skipped during normal `tspec test`, run with `-- --ignored`.
    40→
    41→Manual testing: `cd tests/fixtures/pop-fail && tspec test`.
    42→
    43→Also add unit tests for `print_test_summary` with synthetic
    44→OpResult data (pass/fail/mixed with counts, verify exit codes).
    45→
    46→### Result
    47→
    48→- `tee.rs` — reusable tee_stdout(cmd, filter, suppress) utility
    49→- `cargo_build.rs` — run_cargo returns raw matched lines for Test
    50→  mode; noise suppression for "running 0 tests" blocks
    51→- `cmd/test.rs` — TestResult, parse_test_result_line(),
    52→  parse_test_results(); 0-test guard for single-package mode
    53→- `all.rs` — OpResult.test_counts, per-package counts in summary,
    54→  aggregate footer
    55→- 0-test guard only in single-package mode (workspace fixtures
    56→  legitimately have 0 tests)
    57→
    58→## 20260220 - Remove classify_package and PackageKind
    59→
    60→### Context
    61→
    62→`classify_package()` hardcoded directory conventions (`apps/`, `libs/`,
    63→`tools/`, `*/tests`) specific to one workspace layout. tspec shouldn't
    64→impose layout opinions — cargo metadata already provides `has_binary`,
    65→and the only real behavioral need is excluding build tools (tspec/xt/xtask)
    66→from batch operations.
    67→
    68→### Changes
    69→
    70→**`src/workspace.rs`:**
    71→- Removed `PackageKind` enum (5 variants) and `classify_package()` fn
    72→- Removed `test_members()` method and `is_build_tool` field
    73→- `PackageMember` now has only `name`, `path`, `has_binary`
    74→- `buildable_members()` returns all members (no exclusions)
    75→- `runnable_members()` returns all members with binaries
    76→- Replaced 11 classify tests with 1 `discover_works` test
    77→
    78→**`src/all.rs`:**
    79→- Removed `PackageKind`, `PermissionsExt` imports
    80→- Removed `PackageKind::Test` skip and entire 100-line
    81→  test-binary-discovery block from `test_all()`
    82→- `test_all()` now just iterates `buildable_members()` + `test_package()`
    83→
    84→**`tests/integration_test.rs`:**
    85→- Removed `#[ignore]` from all 16 integration tests — they run
    86→  by default now since they're fast and anyone running `tspec test`
    87→  has the full toolchain
    88→
    89→### Rationale for removing is_build_tool
    90→
    91→The `xt`/`xtask` exclusions were vestigial from the rlibc-x workspace.
    92→Excluding `tspec` from its own `tspec test` was counterproductive —
    93→`tspec test` only showed tspec-build in the summary. No workspace
    94→layout opinions should be baked into tspec.
    95→
    96→### Behavioral changes
    97→
    98→- `tspec test` (all-packages mode) now includes all workspace members
    99→- `runnable_members()` returns any member with a binary (no exclusions)
   100→- Integration tests run by default (no `-- --ignored` needed)
   101→
   102→## 20260223 - Add --manifest-path flag with --mp alias
   103→
   104→### Problem
   105→
   106→tspec currently requires `cd`ing into a project to operate on it.
   107→Adding `--manifest-path` (with `visible_alias = "mp"`) lets users run
   108→`tspec build --mp /path/to/project` from anywhere, matching cargo's
   109→own `--manifest-path` convention but with a shorter alias.
   110→
   111→### Approach: thread project_root through internal APIs
   112→
   113→The core insight: `project_root` is already computed in `main.rs` and
   114→passed to every command's `execute()`. But three helpers re-derive it
   115→from cwd:
   116→
   117→- `resolve_package_arg()` calls `find_project_root()` internally
   118→- `current_package_name()` uses `std::env::current_dir()`
   119→- `WorkspaceInfo::discover()` runs `cargo metadata` from cwd
   120→
   121→Fix: refactor these three to accept `project_root` as a parameter,
   122→then wire `--manifest-path` into `main.rs` to override
   123→`find_project_root()`.
   124→
   125→### Plan (multi-step)
   126→
   127→- **dev1:** Refactor `resolve_package_arg()`, `current_package_name()`,
   128→  and `WorkspaceInfo::discover()` to accept `project_root`. Update all
   129→  callers. No behavior change.
   130→- **dev2:** Add `--manifest-path` / `--mp` global flag to CLI.
   131→  Add `resolve_manifest_path()` to `find_paths.rs`. Wire into
   132→  `main.rs` to override `find_project_root()`.
   133→
   134→### Status
   135→
   136→In progress.
   137→
   138→## 20260219 - Test infrastructure: fixture workspaces and test args
   139→
   140→### Context
   141→
   142→tspec has 255 unit tests but zero integration tests that actually build real packages.
   143→The top todo item is "Add a permanent test workspace for integration testing."
   144→
   145→Three project structures need test coverage:
   146→1. **POP** — Plain Old Package (single Cargo.toml, no workspace)
   147→2. **POP+WS** — POP with embedded workspace (like tspec itself)
   148→3. **POWS** — Pure Old Workspace (workspace root, no package)
   149→
   150→### Plan
   151→
   152→**dev1:** Add test selection flags to `tspec test`:
   153→- `--test <TARGET>` (repeatable) — select specific test targets
   154→- `--names/-n <NAME>...` — filter tests by name (OR-matched substrings via test harness)
   155→- `--list/-l` — list test targets and functions (grouped with counts)
   156→- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing args to test binary (--ignored, --exact, etc.)
   157→All flow through `CargoFlags.extra_args` — no new parameters to `test_package()`/`run_cargo()`.
   158→
   159→**dev2:** POP fixture in `tests/fixtures/pop/` with copy-to-tmpdir helper and `#[ignore]` integration tests.
   160→
   161→**dev3:** POP+WS fixture in `tests/fixtures/pop-ws/` with workspace discovery and cross-package tests.
   162→
   163→**dev4:** POWS fixture in `tests/fixtures/pows/` with all-packages mode tests.
   164→
   165→### Status (20260220)
   166→
   167→**Branch:** `feat-test-fixtures` off `main` (at `a98ebad`)
   168→
   169→**Completed:**
   170→- dev0: `534ce4b` — marker commit, documented -dev0 convention in CLAUDE.md + README.md
   171→- dev1: `28baac3` — test selection flags (--test, --names, --list, --target-names, trailing args)
   172→  - Also added: `extra_args: Vec<String>` to `CargoFlags` in types.rs
   173→  - Also added: workspace-mode --test auto-filters to packages that have the target
   174→  - 268 unit tests, all passing. Installed and verified.
   175→- dev2: `f85a1cb` — POP fixture + integration tests
   176→  - Created `tests/fixtures/pop/` with Cargo.toml, src/main.rs, tspec.ts.toml
   177→  - Added `exclude = ["tests/fixtures"]` to root Cargo.toml `[workspace]`
   178→  - Created `tests/fixture.rs` helper: `copy_fixture(name) -> (TempDir, PathBuf)`
   179→  - Created `tests/integration_test.rs` with 4 `#[ignore]` tests
   180→  - Bug fix: `find_project_root()` now respects workspace `exclude` list
   181→  - Added `is_excluded_from_workspace()` function + 4 unit tests
   182→- dev3: `5c24d4b` — POP+WS fixture
   183→  - Created `tests/fixtures/pop-ws/` with root binary + mylib member
   184→  - Added 6 integration tests (spec load, cargo build, tspec build root/member/all, build from member dir)
   185→  - Bug fix: `classify_package()` now uses paths relative to workspace root
   186→  - Added unit test for path-under-tests-dir classification
   187→- dev4: POWS fixture
   188→  - Created `tests/fixtures/pows/` with workspace-only root (no [package]), pows-app binary, mylib library
   189→  - Added 6 integration tests (cargo build, tspec build all/member/from-member-dir, tspec test all, dot-resolves-to-all)
   190→  - Fix: directory name must match package name for `find_package_dir()` resolution (pows-app/ not app/)
   191→  - All 16 integration tests passing, 268 unit tests passing
   192→
   193→**Remaining:** release (v0.15.0)
   194→
   195→### Future: --manifest-path / --path flag
   196→
   197→Add a way to run tspec against a project without cd'ing into it. Cargo uses `--manifest-path <path/to/Cargo.toml>` (not `--path` — unclear why). We could follow cargo's convention (`--manifest-path`) or use `--path <dir>` for convenience. Would simplify integration tests and general usability. Not blocking dev2-dev4.
   198→
   199→### Documentation TODO
   200→
   201→Where and how to document the test fixtures (decide before release):
   202→- `README.md` — new `## Testing tspec` section documenting when/how to run integration tests
   203→- `tests/README.md` — purpose of the tests directory, overview of unit vs integration tests
   204→- `tests/fixtures/README.md` — purpose of fixtures, how copy_fixture() works
   205→- `tests/fixtures/pop/README.md` (and each fixture) — purpose of that specific fixture
   206→
   207→Open question: should the per-fixture READMEs be the "main" docs that README.md links to, or should README.md be self-contained with the READMEs as secondary?
   208→
   209→Full plan: `.claude/plans/enumerated-booping-curry.md`
