```
     1→# tspec - Translation Spec Build System
     2→
     3→A spec-driven build system for Rust that wraps cargo with configurable target triples, compiler flags, and linker options. Each tspec applies to a Cargo **package** — the unit with a `Cargo.toml`. If different crates need different compilation settings, they should be in separate packages.
     4→
     5→**Status:** Active development. Works with both workspaces and single-package projects (i.e. Plain Old Packages, POPs).
     6→
     7→## Installation
     8→
     9→```bash
    10→cargo install --path .
    11→```
    12→
    13→Or run directly:
    14→```bash
    15→cargo run -- build
    16→```
    17→
    18→## Contributing
    19→
    20→**All non-trivial changes must be on a feature branch** — never commit directly to `main`. This applies even for solo development.
    21→
    22→```bash
    23→git checkout -b feat-my-feature    # Create a branch
    24→# ... make changes, commit ...
    25→git checkout main                  # Switch back
    26→git merge --no-ff feat-my-feature  # Merge with merge commit (or squash-merge)
    27→git push
    28→```
    29→
    30→Branch naming: `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`.
    31→
    32→**Commits:** Conventional commits with the 50/72 rule — subject under 50 chars, body wrapped at 72 (the limit, not a target). Append `, vX.Y.Z` to the subject only on release commits.
    33→
    34→**Versioning:** Single-step changes bump directly (e.g., `0.14.0` → `0.15.0`). Multi-step features use a `-devN` series: `-dev0` for the marker/planning commit, then `-dev1`, `-dev2`, etc. for each code step, dropping the suffix on release.
    35→
    36→**Protect main on GitHub:** Enable branch protection (Settings > Branches) to block force pushes and optionally require PRs.
    37→
    38→For the full feature workflow (version bumps, notes, merge steps, Claude Code conventions), see [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md#feature-workflow).
    39→
    40→## Usage
    41→
    42→```bash
    43→tspec build                                # Build current package (or all in workspace) with tspec.ts.toml if present
    44→tspec build -p myapp                       # Build specific package with tspec.ts.toml if present
    45→tspec build myapp                          # Same, using positional argument
    46→tspec build .                              # Build the package in current directory
    47→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt          # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    48→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt.ts.toml  # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    49→tspec build -p myapp -r -s                 # Build release, strip symbols with tspec.ts.toml if present
    50→tspec run -p myapp                         # Build and run with tspec.ts.toml if present
    51→tspec test -p myapp                        # Build and test with tspec.ts.toml if present
    52→tspec build -w                             # Build all packages (even from inside a package dir)
    53→tspec build -t 'tspec*'                    # Build all packages using matching specs (quote the glob!)
    54→tspec build -t 'tspec-opt*'               # Build all packages with tspec-opt specs only
    55→tspec compare -p myapp                     # Compare binary sizes for a package
    56→tspec compare -p myapp -t *.ts.toml        # Compare using shell-expanded glob
    57→tspec compare                              # Compare all packages (workspace mode)
    58→tspec compare -w                           # Force workspace mode from inside a package
    59→tspec compare -w -f                        # Workspace mode, stop on first failure
    60→tspec -v build .                           # Verbose: show cargo command/env + cargo -v
    61→tspec -vv build .                          # Debug: also show spec details + cargo -vv
    62→tspec -j4 build .                          # Limit cargo to 4 parallel jobs
    63→```
    64→
    65→The `-p` flag or positional argument specifies a package by name or path (defaults to current directory if in a package, otherwise all packages). Paths like `.` are resolved to the actual cargo package name. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` means "all packages."
    66→Use `-w, --workspace` to force all-packages mode even when inside a package directory.
    67→The `-t` flag selects a tspec file; if omitted and `tspec.ts.toml` exists, it's used automatically. In workspace mode (no `-p`, or with `-w`), `-t` patterns are resolved per-package — each sub-package is searched for matching specs, and packages with no matches are skipped.
    68→
    69→**Global flags** apply to all commands and are passed through to cargo: `-v` (cargo `-v`, shows rustc invocations), `-vv` (cargo `-vv`, full command details), and `-j N` (parallel jobs). tspec's own verbose output (command line, env vars, spec resolution) is also controlled by `-v`/`-vv`.
    70→
    71→**Important: quote glob patterns with `-t`.** The shell expands unquoted globs *before* tspec sees them. At a workspace root, `tspec build -t tspec*` may expand to literal filenames from the root directory (or worse, match `target/`), rather than being matched per-package. Always quote: `-t 'tspec*'`. tspec will warn if it detects shell-expanded non-tspec arguments.
    72→
    73→**Note on `cmd` vs `cmd .`:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), while `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata files. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    74→
    75→## tspec Files
    76→
    77→Translation specs are TOML files (conventionally `*.ts.toml`) that configure builds:
    78→
    79→```toml
    80→# tspec.ts.toml - all supported fields shown
    81→
    82→# Top-level high-level options (expand to lower-level cargo/rustc flags)
    83→panic = "abort"                            # "unwind" (default), "abort", "immediate-abort" (nightly)
    84→strip = "symbols"                          # "none" (default), "debuginfo", "symbols"
    85→toolchain = "nightly"                      # override toolchain (e.g., "nightly", "stable", "1.75")
    86→rustflags = ["-C", "relocation-model=static"]  # raw flags passed through to RUSTFLAGS
    87→
    88→[cargo]
    89→profile = "release"                        # "debug" (default), "release", or custom profile
    90→target_triple = "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl"
    91→target_json = "path/to/custom-target.json" # custom target spec (auto-adds -Z json-target-spec)
    92→unstable = ["panic-immediate-abort"]       # -Z flags (nightly only)
    93→target_dir = "{name}"                      # per-spec target dir; supports {name} and {hash} placeholders
    94→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]              # crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly)
    95→
    96→[cargo.config.profile.release]             # nested tables under [cargo.config] become individual
    97→opt-level = "z"                            # --config KEY=VALUE args to cargo; any valid cargo
    98→lto = true                                 # config key works (profile, build, target, etc.)
    99→codegen-units = 1
   100→
   101→[linker]
   102→args = ["-static", "-nostartfiles"]
   103→
   104→[linker.version_script]                    # symbol visibility control (enables --gc-sections)
   105→global = ["_start"]                        # symbols to keep
   106→local = "*"                                # default: "*" (hide everything else)
   107→```
   108→
   109→The top-level `panic` sets both cargo `-Z` and rustc `-C` flags automatically.
   110→
   111→### Key Concepts
   112→
   113→- **One tspec per package** - A tspec applies to all crates (targets) within a Cargo package. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
   114→- **tspec.ts.toml is optional** - Packages without one get plain `cargo build/test`
   115→- **Generated build.rs** - tspec generates temporary build.rs for scoped linker flags
   116→- **Spec comparison** - Compare binary sizes across different specs; always shows both `cargo --release` and `cargo --release-strip` baselines. Supports `-w` for all-packages mode — per-package tables are collected and printed together at the end. With a single package, only its comparison table is shown
   117→
   118→### How Linker Flags Work
   119→
   120→tspec generates a temporary `build.rs` to apply linker flags with binary-scoped directives:
   121→
   122→```rust
   123→// Generated by tspec - deleted after build
   124→fn main() {
   125→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-static");
   126→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-nostdlib");
   127→}
   128→```
   129→
   130→The only other alternative is to add these linker flags to RUSTFLAGS, but that
   131→affects ALL compilations (including dependency build scripts), causing crashes
   132→with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated build.rs scopes flags to just
   133→the binary.
   134→
   135→### Cargo Config Passthrough (`cargo.config`)
   136→
   137→The `[cargo.config]` section is a general-purpose passthrough to cargo's `--config KEY=VALUE` mechanism. Any nested tables under `[cargo.config]` are flattened into dotted keys and passed as `--config` args. This lets you override any [cargo configuration](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html) value at the command line.
   138→
   139→**Profile settings** are the most common use case:
   140→
   141→```toml
   142→[cargo.config.profile.release]
   143→opt-level = "z"
   144→codegen-units = 1
   145→lto = true
   146→```
   147→
   148→This produces: `cargo --config profile.release.opt-level="z" --config profile.release.codegen-units=1 --config profile.release.lto=true`
   149→
   150→**Other cargo config keys** work too:
   151→
   152→```toml
   153→[cargo.config.target.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu]
   154→linker = "clang"
   155→runner = "qemu-x86_64"
   156→```
   157→
   158→**Note:** Do not use `build.rustflags` in `[cargo.config]`. tspec sets the `RUSTFLAGS` env var (from top-level `rustflags`, `panic`, and `strip`), which takes precedence over `--config build.rustflags` per cargo's precedence rules. Use the top-level `rustflags` array instead.
   159→
   160→**Custom profiles:** Any profile defined in your workspace `Cargo.toml` (with `inherits`) can be used. tspec validates that custom profiles exist before invoking cargo. Use `cargo.profile` in the spec or `--profile` on the CLI.
   161→
   162→**Flat dotted keys** also work — TOML treats them equivalently:
   163→
   164→```toml
   165→[cargo.config]
   166→"profile.release.opt-level" = "z"
   167→```
   168→
   169→## tspec Management (ts subcommand)
   170→
   171→Manage spec files without manual TOML editing. All commands that modify files preserve comments and formatting.
   172→
   173→| Command | Description |
   174→|---------|-------------|
   175→| `ts list` | List tspec files found in the workspace or a specific package |
   176→| `ts show` | Display the TOML contents of a tspec file |
   177→| `ts hash` | Print the content hash (used in backup filenames) of a tspec |
   178→| `ts new` | Create a new tspec with defaults, or copy an existing one with `-f` (byte-for-byte) |
   179→| `ts set` | Set a scalar value or replace an entire array |
   180→| `ts unset` | Remove a field (scalar or array) from a tspec entirely |
   181→| `ts add` | Add items to an array field (append or insert at position) |
   182→| `ts remove` | Remove items from an array field (by value or by index) |
   183→| `ts backup` | Create a versioned backup copy (`name-NNN-hash.ts.toml`) |
   184→| `ts restore` | Restore a tspec by copying a versioned backup back to its original name |
   185→
   186→```bash
   187→# List / show / hash
   188→tspec ts list                            # List all tspec files
   189→tspec ts list -p myapp                   # List tspec files for a package
   190→tspec ts show -p myapp                   # Show all tspec contents
   191→tspec ts show -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show specific tspec-opt.ts.toml
   192→tspec ts hash -p myapp                   # Show content hash of tspec.ts.toml
   193→
   194→# Create new tspec files
   195→tspec ts new -p myapp                    # Create tspec.ts.toml with defaults
   196→tspec ts new experiment -p myapp         # Create experiment.ts.toml
   197→tspec ts new opt2 -p myapp -f tspec-opt  # Copy from existing spec (byte-for-byte)
   198→
   199→# Set scalar values
   200→tspec ts set strip symbols -p myapp              # Set a scalar field
   201→tspec ts set cargo.profile release               # No quoting needed
   202→
   203→# Set (replace) entire arrays — each element is a separate arg
   204→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib        # Replace array
   205→tspec ts set cargo.build_std core alloc           # Replace array
   206→tspec ts set rustflags -C relocation-model=static # Replace array
   207→
   208→# Add items to arrays (append by default)
   209→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections        # Append one item
   210→tspec ts add linker.args -nostdlib -pie            # Append multiple items
   211→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles        # Insert at position 0
   212→
   213→# Remove items from arrays
   214→tspec ts remove linker.args -nostdlib              # Remove by value
   215→tspec ts remove linker.args -static -pie           # Remove multiple by value
   216→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args                   # Remove by index
   217→
   218→# Remove a field entirely
   219→tspec ts unset strip -p myapp                      # Remove scalar field
   220→tspec ts unset linker.args -t tspec-opt            # Remove array field
   221→
   222→# Backup and restore (byte-for-byte copies)
   223→tspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Backup tspec.ts.toml → tspec-001-abcd1234.ts.toml
   224→tspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup tspec-opt.ts.toml
   225→tspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup
   226→```
   227→
   228→Backups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.
   229→
   230→`ts set`, `ts unset`, `ts add`, and `ts remove` use `toml_edit` for surgical editing — comments and formatting are preserved.
   231→`ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` use byte-for-byte file copy — comments are preserved exactly.
   232→
   233→### Supported keys
   234→
   235→Keys use two forms: `FIELD` for top-level fields (`panic`, `strip`, `rustflags`) or `TABLE.FIELD` for nested fields (`cargo.profile`, `linker.args`).
   236→
   237→| Key | Type | Values |
   238→|-----|------|--------|
   239→| `panic` | scalar | `unwind`, `abort`, `immediate-abort` |
   240→| `strip` | scalar | `none`, `debuginfo`, `symbols` |
   241→| `toolchain` | scalar | e.g. `nightly`, `stable`, `nightly-2025-01-15`, `1.75` |
   242→| `rustflags` | array | e.g. `-C relocation-model=static` |
   243→| `cargo.profile` | scalar | `debug`, `release`, or any custom profile defined in `Cargo.toml` |
   244→| `cargo.target_triple` | scalar | any string |
   245→| `cargo.target_json` | scalar | any path |
   246→| `cargo.target_dir` | scalar | any string (supports `{name}`, `{hash}`) |
   247→| `cargo.unstable` | array | e.g. `panic-immediate-abort` |
   248→| `cargo.config` | table | arbitrary `--config KEY=VALUE` passthrough; supports nested tables (e.g. `[cargo.config.profile.release]`) |
   249→| `cargo.build_std` | array | e.g. `core alloc` |
   250→| `linker.args` | array | e.g. `-static -nostdlib` |
   251→
   252→### Array operations
   253→
   254→Four orthogonal commands operate on arrays at two levels:
   255→
   256→| Level | Command | Purpose |
   257→|-------|---------|---------|
   258→| Field-level | `ts set` | Replace entire array with new values |
   259→| Field-level | `ts unset` | Remove the array field entirely |
   260→| Item-level | `ts add` | Append items (or insert at position with `-i`) |
   261→| Item-level | `ts remove` | Remove items by value (or by index with `-i`) |
   262→
   263→```bash
   264→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib   # replace all with these
   265→tspec ts unset linker.args                   # clear the field
   266→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections   # append (skips duplicates)
   267→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles  # insert at position
   268→tspec ts remove linker.args -static          # remove by value
   269→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args             # remove by index
   270→```
   271→
   272→Values starting with `-` work without quoting thanks to `allow_hyphen_values`.
   273→Use `--` as an escape hatch if a value collides with a flag like `-p`, `-t`, or `-i`.
   274→
   275→Note: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for "tspec management").
   276→
   277→## Testing
   278→
   279→**Important:** `tspec test` skips `#[ignore]`'d tests by default (same as `cargo test`). Use `--all-tests` to include them:
   280→
   281→```bash
   282→tspec test -p tspec                        # Run tspec tests (skips #[ignore]'d)
   283→tspec test -p tspec --all-tests            # Run ALL tests including #[ignore]'d
   284→tspec test --all-tests                     # Run all workspace tests including #[ignore]'d
   285→```
   286→
   287→```bash
   288→# Basic usage
   289→tspec test -p tspec                        # Run tspec tests
   290→tspec test -p tspec-build                  # Run tspec-build tests
   291→tspec test -w                              # Run all workspace tests
   292→
   293→# Filter by test function name (-n/--names, substring match, OR-matched)
   294→tspec test -p tspec -n flatten_config      # Run tests matching "flatten_config"
   295→tspec test -p tspec -n flatten config      # Run tests matching "flatten" OR "config"
   296→
   297→# Select test target (--test, basename of files in tests/ without .rs)
   298→tspec test --test tspec_test               # Run only tests/tspec_test.rs
   299→tspec test --test foo --test bar           # Run multiple test files
   300→tspec test --test tspec_test -n load_spec  # Combine: target + name filter
   301→
   302→# Discovery
   303→tspec test -p tspec --target-names         # List available names for --test
   304→tspec test -p tspec --list                 # List all test functions grouped by target
   305→tspec test -p tspec -n flatten --list      # List functions matching a name filter
   306→```
   307→
   308→**Test flags:**
   309→- `--all-tests` — include `#[ignore]`'d tests (equivalent to `-- --include-ignored`)
   310→- `-n, --names <FILTER>...` — match anywhere in the qualified test name (e.g., `-n remove` matches `ts_cmd::remove::tests::remove_by_index`; multiple filters are OR-matched)
   311→- `--test <TARGET-NAME>` — run only the named test target (the basename of a file in `tests/` without `.rs`; repeatable). In workspace mode, only packages containing the target are tested. Use `--target-names` to see available names
   312→- `--target-names` — list available target names for `--test`
   313→- `--list` / `-l` — list all test functions grouped by target
   314→- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing arguments to the test binary (e.g., `--ignored`, `--exact`, `--nocapture`)
   315→
   316→**Test summary** (workspace mode) shows per-package counts and an aggregate footer. Note the difference — without `--all-tests`, `#[ignore]`'d tests are skipped:
   317→
   318→```
   319→$ tspec test                               $ tspec test --all-tests
   320→============================================   ============================================
   321→             tspec TEST SUMMARY                             tspec TEST SUMMARY
   322→============================================   ============================================
   323→  Package      Status                           Package      Status
   324→  tspec-build  [PASS]  10 passed                tspec-build  [PASS]  10 passed
   325→  tspec        [PASS]  298 passed               tspec        [PASS]  302 passed
   326→
   327→  Test: 2 packages, 308 passed, 0 failed        Test: 2 packages, 312 passed, 0 failed
   328→============================================   ============================================
   329→```
   330→
   331→In single-package mode, tspec fails (exit 1) when 0 tests ran — this catches filter typos that silently pass. "Running 0 tests" noise blocks are suppressed from output.
   332→
   333→**Fail fixtures** for testing failure output are in `tests/fixtures/pop-fail/` and `tests/fixtures/pows-fail/`. These have intentionally failing tests and can be run manually:
   334→
   335→```bash
   336→cd tests/fixtures/pop-fail && tspec test .    # Single-package failure
   337→cd tests/fixtures/pows-fail && tspec test     # Workspace mixed pass/fail
   338→```
   339→
   340→Integration tests using fail fixtures are `#[ignore]`'d — run them with `--all-tests`:
   341→
   342→```bash
   343→tspec test --all-tests                     # Run everything including fail-fixture tests
   344→tspec test -- --ignored                    # Run ONLY the #[ignore]'d tests
   345→```
   346→
   347→## Project Structure
   348→
   349→```
   350→tspec/                  # Workspace root (also the main tspec package)
   351→  Cargo.toml            # [workspace] + [package] — workspace with root package
   352→  src/
   353→    lib.rs              # Library root, exposes modules
   354→    main.rs             # Entry point, dispatch
   355→    cli.rs              # Clap CLI definitions
   356→    types.rs            # Spec parameter types
   357→    tspec.rs            # Spec loading/saving/hashing
   358→    find_paths.rs       # Project/package/tspec/binary path discovery
   359→    workspace.rs        # Workspace package discovery
   360→    cargo_build.rs      # Unified cargo runner (build + test) + generated build.rs
   361→    tee.rs              # Tee utility: run command, print stdout live, collect matching lines
   362→    run.rs              # Run command implementation
   363→    compare.rs          # Compare command (size comparison)
   364→    all.rs              # Batch operations (build_all, run_all, test_all, compare_all)
   365→    cmd/                # Command implementations (one file per command)
   366→    ts_cmd/             # Tspec management subcommands
   367→    binary.rs           # Binary operations (strip, size)
   368→  tests/
   369→    data/               # Test fixtures (TOML specs)
   370→    fixtures/           # Project fixtures for integration tests
   371→      pop/              # Plain Old Package
   372→      pop-ws/           # POP with embedded workspace
   373→      pows/             # Pure Old Workspace (no root package)
   374→      pop-fail/         # POP with failing tests
   375→      pows-fail/        # POWS with failing tests (mixed pass/fail)
   376→    integration_test.rs # Integration tests (fail-fixture tests are #[ignore]'d)
   377→    tspec_test.rs       # Spec loading tests
   378→  tspec-build/          # Library crate for build.rs integration
   379→    src/lib.rs          # Reads TSPEC_SPEC_FILE env var at build time
   380→  notes/                # Design docs and todo tracking
   381→```
   382→

     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→**tspec** is a spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust that sits on top of cargo. It configures builds at the **package** level (one tspec per Cargo package) via translation spec files (TOML-based) with support for target triples, compiler flags, linker options, and high-level build options like panic strategies and symbol stripping. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
     8→
     9→**Repository structure:** This is a Cargo workspace with a root package (`tspec`) and one member (`tspec-build`). The root `Cargo.toml` has both `[workspace]` and `[package]` sections — no subdirectory restructuring needed.
    10→
    11→- **tspec** — the main CLI binary
    12→- **tspec-build** — a library crate that packages can use in their `build.rs` to read tspec settings at build time via the `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE` env var
    13→
    14→## Build Commands — Dogfood tspec
    15→
    16→**Always use `tspec` itself for development.** Never use bare `cargo test`, `cargo clippy`, `cargo fmt`, or `cargo install` — use the tspec equivalents. This is how we catch real-world issues.
    17→
    18→```bash
    19→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tspec tests (NEVER cargo test)
    20→tspec test -p tspec-build      # Run tspec-build tests
    21→tspec clippy                   # Run lints (NEVER cargo clippy)
    22→tspec fmt --check              # Check formatting (NEVER cargo fmt --check)
    23→tspec install --path .         # Install from local source (NEVER cargo install)
    24→```
    25→
    26→**After code changes pass tests, immediately install the new version** with `tspec install --path .` so subsequent commands use the latest binary. Do not defer installation — the sooner we run the real binary, the sooner we catch issues.
    27→
    28→## Architecture
    29→
    30→### Execute Trait Pattern
    31→
    32→Commands implement the `Execute` trait:
    33→
    34→```rust
    35→pub trait Execute {
    36→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    37→}
    38→
    39→// Helper for simple cargo passthroughs
    40→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(subcommand: &str, args: &[OsString], project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>
    41→```
    42→
    43→- **Simple passthroughs** (Clean, Clippy, Fmt): Use `execute_cargo_subcommand()` helper
    44→- **Custom commands** (Build, Run, Test, Compare, Install, Version): Implement own logic
    45→
    46→Command structs derive `clap::Args` and are used directly in the `Commands` enum:
    47→```rust
    48→enum Commands {
    49→    Clean(CleanCmd),
    50→    Test(TestCmd),
    51→    // ...
    52→}
    53→```
    54→
    55→### Key Modules
    56→
    57→- `cmd/` - Command implementations (one file per command)
    58→  - `mod.rs` - Execute trait, `execute_cargo_subcommand` helper, `resolve_package_arg()` (resolves paths like "." to package names), `current_package_name()` (workspace-aware), re-exports
    59→  - `build.rs`, `clean.rs`, `clippy.rs`, `compare.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `install.rs`, `run.rs`, `test.rs`, `version.rs` - Individual commands
    60→- `cli.rs` - Clap CLI definitions
    61→- `types.rs` - Spec types (CargoConfig, LinkerConfig)
    62→- `tspec.rs` - Spec loading/saving/hashing, `copy_spec_snapshot()` for byte-for-byte backups
    63→- `cargo_build.rs` - Package build orchestration with spec application
    64→- `workspace.rs` - Workspace package discovery
    65→- `all.rs` - Batch operations (build_all, test_all, run_all)
    66→- `ts_cmd/edit.rs` - `toml_edit` helpers: field registry, `set_field()`, `unset_field()`, `add_items()`, `remove_items_by_value()`, `remove_item_by_index()`, validation
    67→- `ts_cmd/set.rs` - `ts set` command (scalar or replace entire array)
    68→- `ts_cmd/unset.rs` - `ts unset` command (remove field entirely)
    69→- `ts_cmd/add.rs` - `ts add` command (append or insert at position)
    70→- `ts_cmd/remove.rs` - `ts remove` command (by value or by index)
    71→- `tspec-build/` - Library crate for build.rs integration (reads `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE` env var)
    72→
    73→### Three Write Strategies
    74→
    75→| Operation | Strategy | Why |
    76→|---|---|---|
    77→| load, hash, build | serde (`toml`) | Need typed `Spec` struct for build logic |
    78→| set, unset, add, remove | `toml_edit` (`DocumentMut`) | Surgical edit preserving comments/formatting |
    79→| backup, restore, new -f | raw `fs::copy` | Exact byte-for-byte preservation |
    80→
    81→### Translation Spec Structure
    82→
    83→Specs are TOML files (`*.ts.toml`) with top-level fields and two sections:
    84→- Top-level: `panic`, `strip`, `rustflags`
    85→- `[cargo]` - profile, target_triple, target_json, unstable, target_dir, build_std, config
    86→- `[linker]` - args, version_script
    87→
    88→## Conventions
    89→
    90→- **Rust Edition:** 2024
    91→- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:). Follow the 50/72 rule: subject line under 50 chars, body wrapped at 72 (that's the limit, not a target — don't wrap prematurely). Append `, vX.Y.Z` to the subject only on release commits — e.g. `feat: Add glob support for -t, v0.11.3`. No version tags on intermediate `-devN` commits.
    92→- **Naming:** POP (Plain Old Package) refers to single-package projects (no workspace); tspec treats them as trivial workspaces
    93→- **Granularity:** tspec operates at the Cargo package level, not the crate level. "Package" = directory with Cargo.toml. A package may contain multiple crates (targets), but they all share one tspec.
    94→- **Package argument pattern:** All commands accept both `-p <name-or-path>` and a positional `<PACKAGE>` argument. Paths (like `.`) are resolved to the actual cargo package name via `resolve_package_arg()`. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` resolves to None (all-packages mode). Resolution order: positional > `-p` > current directory > all packages.
    95→- **`cmd` vs `cmd .` for passthroughs:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), but `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    96→- **Markdown refs:** Multiple references use `[1],[2]` not `[1,2]` or `[1][2]` (both break in markdown)
    97→
    98→## Feature Workflow
    99→
   100→**All non-trivial changes must be on a feature branch — never commit directly to main.** Even "quick" changes tend to grow; start on a branch and it's always safe to amend, rebase, or force-push without risk to main.
   101→
   102→**Before starting feature or fix work:**
   103→1. Create a branch: `git checkout -b <type>-<short-description>` (e.g., `fix-compare-optional-p`)
   104→2. Create a dated entry in `notes/chores-N.md` with context and plan
   105→3. Update `notes/todo.md` to move items to In Progress
   106→4. Bump version in `Cargo.toml` (always required — use `X.Y.Z-dev0` for multi-step marker commit, direct bump for single-step)
   107→5. For plans: recommend single-step (one commit) vs multi-step (`-devN` series) and get user approval
   108→6. Commit the above as a `chore:` marker commit (`v0.X.Y-dev0`) before starting code changes
   109→7. Bump to `-dev1` with the first code commit, then `-dev2`, etc. for each subsequent step
   110→8. Use todo list to track progress during implementation
   111→
   112→**On completion:**
   113→1. Update `notes/chores-N.md` with result
   114→2. Move items from In Progress to Done in `notes/todo.md`
   115→3. Remove `-dev` from version in `Cargo.toml`
   116→4. Commit as a release chore (include `Cargo.lock` if changed)
   117→5. User merges branch to main (Claude cannot do checkouts/merges)
   118→
   119→**Branch merge workflow (user performs after Claude's final commit):**
   120→1. Claude makes the final commit on the branch
   121→2. User `/exit`s Claude (optional)
   122→3. User amends `.claude/` files on the branch and pushes
   123→4. User merges the branch to main
   124→5. User pushes main and deletes the branch
   125→6. User restarts Claude (if exited) or continues the conversation
   126→
   127→**Branch naming:** `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`
   128→
   129→**Force-push safety:** Force-pushing is acceptable on feature branches (use `--force-with-lease` when possible). Never force-push to main — main should have branch protection enabled on GitHub.
   130→
   131→## Verification Workflow
   132→
   133→**After code changes, run verification and install immediately:**
   134→```bash
   135→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tspec tests to verify changes with "old" binary; if all pass:
   136→tspec test -p tspec-build      # Run tspec-build tests
   137→tspec install --path .         # Install ASAP so we dogfood the new binary
   138→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tests again with the new binary to catch issues early
   139→tspec clippy
   140→tspec fmt --check
   141→```
   142→
   143→**After committing code, remind about .claude/ files and wait:**
   144→```
   145→Committed abc123.
   146→
   147→Remember to commit .claude/ session files.
   148→```
   149→After the reminder, **wait for the user to respond** before starting the next task. The user needs time to amend `.claude/` files into the commit. Do not immediately create branches, bump versions, or begin new work.
   150→
   151→**On next prompt after a commit+reminder:** Check `git log -1 --name-only` to see if `.claude/` was included in a commit after the code commit. If not, ask: "Did you forget to commit .claude sessions?"
   152→
   153→## Git Operations Claude Cannot Perform
   154→
   155→Due to circular references with `.claude/` session files (which record Claude's behavior), Claude cannot perform these git operations:
   156→
   157→- `git checkout` - switching branches affects session files
   158→- `git merge` - merging affects session files
   159→- `git rebase` - rebasing affects session files
   160→- Amending commits with `.claude/` files
   161→
   162→The user must perform these operations manually. Claude can only make commits (excluding `.claude/` files) and remind the user to handle the rest.
   163→
```
